- [[MT172 - The Market's Biggest Warning Signs Right Now with Todd Sohn - The Real Eisman Playbook Ep#^MT172-C05-3|MT172-C05-3]] — Cumulative mutual-fund flows since 1984 are negative (about -5.1tn, all from active equity funds) while ETFs absorb the flows, so assets in mutual funds depend on market gains.
- [[MT172 - The Market's Biggest Warning Signs Right Now with Todd Sohn - The Real Eisman Playbook Ep#^MT172-C06-1|MT172-C06-1]] — Money is rotating from active mutual funds to cheaper, transparent ETFs including active ETFs.
- [[MT172 - The Market's Biggest Warning Signs Right Now with Todd Sohn - The Real Eisman Playbook Ep#^MT172-C06-3|MT172-C06-3]] — International diversification is not a fix because the US index is so large that single-country caps (Korea, Italy) are small compared with S&P-tracking money.
- [[MT172 - The Market's Biggest Warning Signs Right Now with Todd Sohn - The Real Eisman Playbook Ep#^MT172-C11-3|MT172-C11-3]] — Staples fell from about 19% to 4.5% of the S&P and now lose allocation to covered-call, buffered and income ETFs, so they only attract flows in a market shock.
- [[MT172 - The Market's Biggest Warning Signs Right Now with Todd Sohn - The Real Eisman Playbook Ep#^MT172-C11-X2|MT172-C11-X2]] — Illustration of concentration: Nvidia alone is about twice the whole consumer staples sector (4.5% of the S&P), and Microsoft, Alphabet and Amazon are each also larger, which is why the staples sector barely matters for the cap-weighted index.
- [[MT172 - The Market's Biggest Warning Signs Right Now with Todd Sohn - The Real Eisman Playbook Ep#^MT172-C13-X1|MT172-C13-X1]] — Eisman's synthesis: Meta, Oracle, Microsoft and Amazon look weak because of software exposure to AI and AI capital intensity, while the best tech groups (semiconductors, equipment and power such as GEV) are those not exposed to that, so the market is effectively one AI-hardware theme.

## [[MT173 - The Negative Crisis Beta and CTA's Hidden Market Timing Skills - Systematic Investor 302]]
- [[MT173 - The Negative Crisis Beta and CTA's Hidden Market Timing Skills - Systematic Investor 302#^MT173-C04-2|MT173-C04-2]] — Alternative commodity markets driven by producers and consumers have low cross-dependence and add more diversification than traditional speculative markets; macro markets however provide crisis alpha.
- [[MT173 - The Negative Crisis Beta and CTA's Hidden Market Timing Skills - Systematic Investor 302#^MT173-C16-1|MT173-C16-1]] — Time-series carry in bonds has a 67% correlation to bond beta because curves are mostly upward sloping; cross-sectional carry is more beta-neutral.

## [[MT174 - The Next Evolution of Trend Following ft. Nick Baltas - Systematic Investor - Ep.410]]
- [[MT174 - The Next Evolution of Trend Following ft. Nick Baltas - Systematic Investor - Ep.410#^MT174-C02-1|MT174-C02-1]] — The frontier AI model changes every few weeks (versus months last year), making firm-level AI integration and tool choice hard; using a version one step behind captures most of the benefit.
- [[MT174 - The Next Evolution of Trend Following ft. Nick Baltas - Systematic Investor - Ep.410#^MT174-C07-1|MT174-C07-1]] — Graham Capital finds beyond 70-80 liquid markets, adding less liquid macro markets (currencies, EM equities, swaps) adds little, but exotic commodities, including Chinese ones, still add return up to more than 100 markets, with a liquidity hump.
- [[MT174 - The Next Evolution of Trend Following ft. Nick Baltas - Systematic Investor - Ep.410#^MT174-C10-X2|MT174-C10-X2]] — At the Goldman-hosted research conference machine learning, nonlinear models and LLM-based sentiment and thematic mapping appeared in almost every topic (portfolio construction, equity forecasting), showing AI/ML tools are now standard in financial economics research.
- [[MT174 - The Next Evolution of Trend Following ft. Nick Baltas - Systematic Investor - Ep.410#^MT174-C12-X1|MT174-C12-X1]] — Since COVID investment theses have shifted toward themes (the stay-at-home theme being one example), with banks building thematic baskets that hedge funds use for active bets or hedges; this flow into themes is a driver of the rising within-theme correlation.

## [[MT175 - The Overlooked Markets Creating Better Trend Opportunities - Open Interest - Ep.21]]
- [[MT175 - The Overlooked Markets Creating Better Trend Opportunities - Open Interest - Ep.21#^MT175-C01-1|MT175-C01-1]] — Cocoa in 2024 delivered the biggest commodity trend after twenty years without one, but Gresham avoids it as too financialized, illustrating that trend can appear in markets without a prior trend record.
- [[MT175 - The Overlooked Markets Creating Better Trend Opportunities - Open Interest - Ep.21#^MT175-C02-2|MT175-C02-2]] — Financialized markets (FX, indices, fixed income) collapse to one in macro shocks, while less financialized commodities with structural differences stay resilient and diversifying.
- [[MT175 - The Overlooked Markets Creating Better Trend Opportunities - Open Interest - Ep.21#^MT175-C03-X2|MT175-C03-X2]] — With capacity discipline an alternative-commodity book taps distinct macro risk factors (biofuels and the green transition, uranium and nuclear power for AI data centres, freight routes and onshoring, tariff wars), whereas a manager with about 10bn would look like a standard commodity allocation inside a broader portfolio.
- [[MT175 - The Overlooked Markets Creating Better Trend Opportunities - Open Interest - Ep.21#^MT175-C10-X1|MT175-C10-X1]] — Alternative-commodity trend was insulated from the April 2025 Liberation Day macro shock: the book was up for the month, consistent with niche commodities following their own supply-demand drivers rather than the financial risk-on/risk-off factor.
- [[MT175 - The Overlooked Markets Creating Better Trend Opportunities - Open Interest - Ep.21#^MT175-C12-X2|MT175-C12-X2]] — Why commodity trend hedges inflation: inflation is measured as goods prices and goods are commodities, so commodity prices and inflation are two sides of the same coin, which gives commodity trend positive convexity to both inflation and deflation regimes.

## [[MT176 - The Paradox of High-Volatility Alternatives - Systematic Investor 313]]
- [[MT176 - The Paradox of High-Volatility Alternatives - Systematic Investor 313#^MT176-C02-X1|MT176-C02-X1]] — Skeptical counterview to Draghi's plan: EU-level initiatives lose energy and money to bureaucracy, so a large European investment programme may not deliver the growth it promises.
- [[MT176 - The Paradox of High-Volatility Alternatives - Systematic Investor 313#^MT176-C03-1|MT176-C03-1]] — Rogoff expects periodic supply shifts that make inflation higher and more volatile, so the post-2022 regime has higher macro volatility than the prior decades.
- [[MT176 - The Paradox of High-Volatility Alternatives - Systematic Investor 313#^MT176-C03-X1|MT176-C03-X1]] — German growth after the Schroeder reforms of the 2000s rested on productivity gains, outsourcing to Eastern Europe and cheap Russian energy, a tailwind that has since reversed and is now part of Europe's weakness.
- [[MT176 - The Paradox of High-Volatility Alternatives - Systematic Investor 313#^MT176-C04-3|MT176-C04-3]] — Labour bargaining power has swung back from employers toward workers (shrinking labour force, behavioural change), which is a structural source of wage and inflation pressure.
- [[MT176 - The Paradox of High-Volatility Alternatives - Systematic Investor 313#^MT176-C15-X1|MT176-C15-X1]] — Managed accounts let institutions obtain high volatility cheaply, but individuals and family offices lack the infrastructure and face tax-reporting complexity for each component, so pooled high-volatility funds are the practical route to a more efficient alternatives sleeve.
- [[MT176 - The Paradox of High-Volatility Alternatives - Systematic Investor 313#^MT176-C16-3|MT176-C16-3]] — CTA volatility of 20-25% was normal in 1990 when the industry was under $10bn; institutionalisation from 2000 and AUM growth to about $400bn pushed managers to lower leverage, which also let them manage more money.
- [[MT176 - The Paradox of High-Volatility Alternatives - Systematic Investor 313#^MT176-C17-X1|MT176-C17-X1]] — Dunne sees US investors beginning to shift toward higher-volatility, more capital-efficient alternative products, which he links to higher interest rates.

## [[MT177 - The Secrets of Quantitative Investing - ft. Simon Judes - Top Traders Unplugged 139]]
- [[MT177 - The Secrets of Quantitative Investing - ft. Simon Judes - Top Traders Unplugged 139#^MT177-C03-2|MT177-C03-2]] — Winton's alpha comes from a mixture of behavioural, structural and microstructure sources depending on strategy, so it is hard to say who is on the other side of the trades.
- [[MT177 - The Secrets of Quantitative Investing - ft. Simon Judes - Top Traders Unplugged 139#^MT177-C04-3|MT177-C04-3]] — Winton responded to lower trend Sharpe expectations by splitting into pure trend, diversified CTA and multi-strategy products rather than abandoning trend.
- [[MT177 - The Secrets of Quantitative Investing - ft. Simon Judes - Top Traders Unplugged 139#^MT177-C13-1|MT177-C13-1]] — Sharpe scales with the square root of the effective number of markets given correlations; 50-60 markets might be only 8-10 independent, so adding genuinely different markets adds a lot.
- [[MT177 - The Secrets of Quantitative Investing - ft. Simon Judes - Top Traders Unplugged 139#^MT177-C13-2|MT177-C13-2]] — Alternative markets outperformed major markets over the last decade, including the post-crisis period when mainstream trend struggled; European gas and power gave huge trends (gas up tenfold).
- [[MT177 - The Secrets of Quantitative Investing - ft. Simon Judes - Top Traders Unplugged 139#^MT177-C14-X2|MT177-C14-X2]] — Chinese futures are accessed onshore, through internationalised exchange contracts, via swaps and via the QFI route, an execution method for reaching a diversifying market that others cannot easily trade.
- [[MT177 - The Secrets of Quantitative Investing - ft. Simon Judes - Top Traders Unplugged 139#^MT177-C19-X1|MT177-C19-X1]] — Lower trading costs and lower fees are partial offsets to the decline in gross trend Sharpe, so the investor's net outlook is less negative than the 0.5 gross estimate suggests.

## [[MT178 - The Strategy That Thrives When Markets Panic - Systematic Investor - Ep.403]]
- [[MT178 - The Strategy That Thrives When Markets Panic - Systematic Investor - Ep.403#^MT178-C04-2|MT178-C04-2]] — Passive investing and index concentration: 10 companies now make up 40% of the S&P 500, a structural concentration risk.
- [[MT178 - The Strategy That Thrives When Markets Panic - Systematic Investor - Ep.403#^MT178-C04-X1|MT178-C04-X1]] — Wind and solar generated more electricity worldwide than natural gas for the first time (April 2026), an energy-mix shift that creates an alternative to gas-driven power and is a structural driver for energy trends.
- [[MT178 - The Strategy That Thrives When Markets Panic - Systematic Investor - Ep.403#^MT178-C11-X1|MT178-C11-X1]] — For most of the host's career geopolitical risks were sporadic and isolated, but the last five years brought a regime in which geopolitical risk is persistent, which is why its effect on trend following needs to be studied.
- [[MT178 - The Strategy That Thrives When Markets Panic - Systematic Investor - Ep.403#^MT178-C14-X1|MT178-C14-X1]] — In months of extreme geopolitical-risk moves equities were not a main contributor to managed futures returns, so the gains came from commodities and fixed income (inflation channel) and timing equities in these events is hard, as in the current conflict.

## [[MT179 - The Trader Who Never Spoke...Until Now - Open Interest - Ep.19]]
- [[MT179 - The Trader Who Never Spoke...Until Now - Open Interest - Ep.19#^MT179-C08-2|MT179-C08-2]] — The hedger-opposite method stopped being reproducible because hedgers now have OTC and options routes to lay off risk, so futures positions no longer capture their flows.
- [[MT179 - The Trader Who Never Spoke...Until Now - Open Interest - Ep.19#^MT179-C18-1|MT179-C18-1]] — Smaller funds can trade small orthogonal markets (lumber, orange juice, cotton, cocoa, sugar) that a $10bn fund cannot, giving more diversification and less noise than large funds concentrated in rates and equities.
- [[MT179 - The Trader Who Never Spoke...Until Now - Open Interest - Ep.19#^MT179-C19-X2|MT179-C19-X2]] — Chinese futures markets are very liquid, large and offer portfolio qualities (different contracts) not found on CME, but are difficult for US persons to reach; there are legitimate routes, making them a source of diversification.

## [[MT180 - The Trend Following ETF Revolution - Systematic Investor 298]]
- [[MT180 - The Trend Following ETF Revolution - Systematic Investor 298#^MT180-C03-1|MT180-C03-1]] — US regulatory changes (the ETF Rule and the derivatives rule) in 2020 opened the door to futures-based ETFs; systematic trend ETF assets grew from about $220m to $1.45bn and non-traditional ETFs from $20bn to $150bn since 2020.
- [[MT180 - The Trend Following ETF Revolution - Systematic Investor 298#^MT180-C04-2|MT180-C04-2]] — Of about $350bn in managed futures only about 5% is retail, so ETFs and model portfolios could broaden access to diversifying return streams.
- [[MT180 - The Trend Following ETF Revolution - Systematic Investor 298#^MT180-C05-2|MT180-C05-2]] — In the last three years, with higher rates and inflation, bond volatility is higher and the stock-bond correlation is positive, so 60/40 provides less diversification than in the prior 40 years.
- [[MT180 - The Trend Following ETF Revolution - Systematic Investor 298#^MT180-C05-X2|MT180-C05-X2]] — The roughly $350bn managed futures AUM figure has been flat for a very long time and about half of it is Bridgewater, so the industry has not penetrated institutional portfolios as much as the headline implies.
- [[MT180 - The Trend Following ETF Revolution - Systematic Investor 298#^MT180-C06-1|MT180-C06-1]] — A return to zero interest rates, where buying stocks and bonds suffices, is unlikely, so alternatives are a natural choice, and ETF innovation can make them available to retail investors.
- [[MT180 - The Trend Following ETF Revolution - Systematic Investor 298#^MT180-C08-X1|MT180-C08-X1]] — Excluding Bridgewater the managed futures space is roughly 80% institutional (about 95% including it), so retail penetration is larger than the 5% headline but ETF-style product governance is still unfamiliar to the industry.
- [[MT180 - The Trend Following ETF Revolution - Systematic Investor 298#^MT180-C09-X1|MT180-C09-X1]] — Managers entering the ETF space have three routes: replicate their full strategy in the wrapper, offer a watered-down version trading fewer, more liquid markets, or use a replication approach that infers exposure from historical manager data.
- [[MT180 - The Trend Following ETF Revolution - Systematic Investor 298#^MT180-C09-X2|MT180-C09-X2]] — When ETFs hide the end investor, education must run through adviser networks because the manager cannot talk to the clients directly, while institutions will still do full due diligence.
- [[MT180 - The Trend Following ETF Revolution - Systematic Investor 298#^MT180-C11-2|MT180-C11-2]] — ETF constraints (fewer markets, slow signals because of creation-redemption) give industry-like exposure but performance diverges from classic CTAs depending on whether trends are in big markets (gold, yen, equities) or idiosyncratic ones.
- [[MT180 - The Trend Following ETF Revolution - Systematic Investor 298#^MT180-C11-X1|MT180-C11-X1]] — A classic managed futures fund can trade 100+ markets with many model types (including machine learning), making it a best-ideas product, whereas an ETF is limited to fewer liquid markets and slower signals but still captures the main macro themes.
- [[MT180 - The Trend Following ETF Revolution - Systematic Investor 298#^MT180-C12-X1|MT180-C12-X1]] — Without incentive fees a manager's revenue depends only on AUM, which pushes research toward scalable (liquid, slower) models and more markets rather than the highest possible return.
- [[MT180 - The Trend Following ETF Revolution - Systematic Investor 298#^MT180-C14-X1|MT180-C14-X1]] — AI adds new tools for managers' research processes but also raises barriers to entry because the research is expensive, favouring larger managers.

## [[MT181 - The Two Tribes of Trend - Systematic Investor - Ep.351]]
- [[MT181 - The Two Tribes of Trend - Systematic Investor - Ep.351#^MT181-C02-3|MT181-C02-3]] — Idiosyncratic trend followers treat each market as independent and trade time-series momentum market by market, the traditional cut-losses-short approach of the 70s to 90s.
- [[MT181 - The Two Tribes of Trend - Systematic Investor - Ep.351#^MT181-C02-4|MT181-C02-4]] — Correlation-conscious trend followers look at groupings, asset classes and complexes (cross-sectional view) and manage portfolio correlation and volatility targeting; the industry split into these two styles in the early 2000s.
- [[MT181 - The Two Tribes of Trend - Systematic Investor - Ep.351#^MT181-C03-2|MT181-C03-2]] — Correlation-conscious trend following did very well from 2008 to 2020 because globalization and coordinated central bank policy made markets more causally correlated, which cross-sectional grouping could exploit.
- [[MT181 - The Two Tribes of Trend - Systematic Investor - Ep.351#^MT181-C03-3|MT181-C03-3]] — After 2020 deglobalization, fragmentation and loss of central bank coordination made assets less correlated, favouring idiosyncratic trends (cocoa, soybeans, gold) and hurting the large correlation-conscious managers.
- [[MT181 - The Two Tribes of Trend - Systematic Investor - Ep.351#^MT181-C07-1|MT181-C07-1]] — Mini, micro and nano futures (about 1/10, 1/100, 1/1000 of a standard lot) plus institutional-grade software let retail traders reach the diversification once needing $1-2 million.

## [[MT182 - The VUCA World Is Here - Systematic Investor - Ep.398]]
- [[MT182 - The VUCA World Is Here - Systematic Investor - Ep.398#^MT182-C01-X1|MT182-C01-X1]] — The current environment is a VUCA world (volatility, uncertainty, complexity, ambiguity) in uncharted territory, and the practical question for a quant investor is how to navigate it, which frames the later argument that ambiguity favours trends.
- [[MT182 - The VUCA World Is Here - Systematic Investor - Ep.398#^MT182-C02-X1|MT182-C02-X1]] — Allocators have become steadily more sophisticated and demand more from managers, so competition is both across firms and in meeting investor expectations, which are now high.
- [[MT182 - The VUCA World Is Here - Systematic Investor - Ep.398#^MT182-C05-2|MT182-C05-2]] — Most larger CTAs have moved from breakout triggers to continuous signals, while some smaller managers use breakouts with static position sizing and have had outsized returns in 2026.
- [[MT182 - The VUCA World Is Here - Systematic Investor - Ep.398#^MT182-C07-1|MT182-C07-1]] — A persistent supply-shock inflation regime resembles the 1970s and produces extended price trends across many markets, including fertilizer, propane, butane and refined products affected by the closed Straits of Hormuz.
- [[MT182 - The VUCA World Is Here - Systematic Investor - Ep.398#^MT182-C10-X1|MT182-C10-X1]] — Institutional allocators choose trend following against all other hedge fund strategies that might diversify, not only against other trend followers, so its case must be made relative to the whole alternatives menu.
- [[MT182 - The VUCA World Is Here - Systematic Investor - Ep.398#^MT182-C14-X1|MT182-C14-X1]] — Pure trend-following managers are becoming rarer as most add other strategy types, a drift the host expects may leave even fewer pure trend followers in five years, though it could reverse.

## [[MT183 - The Volatility Apocalypse - Systematic Investor 303]]
- [[MT183 - The Volatility Apocalypse - Systematic Investor 303#^MT183-C05-1|MT183-C05-1]] — Market breadth stays informative about medium-to-long-term fragility (1-2 years) but is no longer a reliable trading signal over weeks to a quarter, because structural vol flows now dominate.
- [[MT183 - The Volatility Apocalypse - Systematic Investor 303#^MT183-C05-2|MT183-C05-2]] — Nvidia, now the biggest company, is the epicenter of a Taiwan risk judged by the guest as a coin flip over two to three years, which could cause a 30% market downturn, so narrow breadth creates fragility.
- [[MT183 - The Volatility Apocalypse - Systematic Investor 303#^MT183-C11-1|MT183-C11-1]] — Millennial-and-younger voters are populist because they lost out to asset inflation; this drives anti-incumbent votes, protectionism and inflation (a flat tax), as in the 1964-82 period, and will persist for years.
- [[MT183 - The Volatility Apocalypse - Systematic Investor 303#^MT183-C11-X1|MT183-C11-X1]] — Millennials have risen from about 40% to 45% of household formation (wealth creation) in two years and gain share every four years as baby boomers die off, which sets a long timeline for the populist, anti-incumbent political shift.
- [[MT183 - The Volatility Apocalypse - Systematic Investor 303#^MT183-C16-2|MT183-C16-2]] — Own long-dated gold calls for ten years because implied vol was low and realized vol rises in structurally inflationary, protectionist, populist periods; the 1968-82 episode saw dramatic vol in bonds, FX and gold.
- [[MT183 - The Volatility Apocalypse - Systematic Investor 303#^MT183-C16-X1|MT183-C16-X1]] — International reserve managers are shifting from dollars and Treasuries towards gold, which supports gold structurally alongside dollar-role and global fragmentation arguments.
- [[MT183 - The Volatility Apocalypse - Systematic Investor 303#^MT183-C16-X2|MT183-C16-X2]] — A fragmented world of competing blocs and economic warfare creates massive flows and realized volatility in bonds, FX, dollar and gold, and gold is the biggest beneficiary, as in the 1968-82 period.

## [[MT184 - The War in Ukraine, Relations With China & Wider Geopolitics (With General David Petraeus)]]
- [[MT184 - The War in Ukraine, Relations With China & Wider Geopolitics (With General David Petraeus)#^MT184-C02-1|MT184-C02-1]] — Ukraine's mobilization, training and equipment have given it a larger and more capable army in the field than Russia's, and the front has been static for months around Bakhmut with Russia taking heavy losses.
- [[MT184 - The War in Ukraine, Relations With China & Wider Geopolitics (With General David Petraeus)#^MT184-C02-X1|MT184-C02-X1]] — Russian casualties in the first 10-11 months were at least six to nine times those of nearly ten years in Afghanistan (some estimates up to 100,000 versus about 13,000), yet Putin seems unconcerned, so battlefield losses alone do not force him to recognise the war as unsustainable.
- [[MT184 - The War in Ukraine, Relations With China & Wider Geopolitics (With General David Petraeus)#^MT184-C03-2|MT184-C03-2]] — The speaker's pre-invasion call that Russia would not take Kyiv rested on watching Ukrainian forces improve since 2014 through Western training, intelligence and anti-tank weapons.
- [[MT184 - The War in Ukraine, Relations With China & Wider Geopolitics (With General David Petraeus)#^MT184-C04-1|MT184-C04-1]] — Russian forces have good rail logistics but only a fraction of the off-rail logistics of Western armies and have not combined arms effects, so their offensives were ineptly planned.
- [[MT184 - The War in Ukraine, Relations With China & Wider Geopolitics (With General David Petraeus)#^MT184-C04-X2|MT184-C04-X2]] — Western allies train whole units and wargame offensives, not just individual soldiers, which raises Ukrainian effectiveness, although complex systems like Patriot and infantry fighting vehicles take time to field.
- [[MT184 - The War in Ukraine, Relations With China & Wider Geopolitics (With General David Petraeus)#^MT184-C05-X1|MT184-C05-X1]] — Russia's partial mobilisation drove more men to emigrate than reported for service, an indicator of weak popular support and labour flight that undercuts its larger population as an advantage.
- [[MT184 - The War in Ukraine, Relations With China & Wider Geopolitics (With General David Petraeus)#^MT184-C07-1|MT184-C07-1]] — Ukraine gives only glimpses of future war; with drones, long-range precision munitions and ubiquitous surveillance, big manned platforms (carriers, F-35s, tanks) become vulnerable, pushing militaries toward many small unmanned, algorithm-piloted systems.
- [[MT184 - The War in Ukraine, Relations With China & Wider Geopolitics (With General David Petraeus)#^MT184-C07-X1|MT184-C07-X1]] — Competition pushes towards autonomy: a force that keeps a human in each decision loop will lose to the opposing machine acting at machine speed, so the human role shifts to designing the algorithm and authorising it.
- [[MT184 - The War in Ukraine, Relations With China & Wider Geopolitics (With General David Petraeus)#^MT184-C08-X1|MT184-C08-X1]] — Cyberspace is a new battlefield domain where conflict is already daily and will intensify, aimed at disrupting the networks linking sensors, shooters and autonomous systems.
- [[MT184 - The War in Ukraine, Relations With China & Wider Geopolitics (With General David Petraeus)#^MT184-C08-X2|MT184-C08-X2]] — Putin's invasion pushed Finland and Sweden into NATO, which is geostrategically important because it makes the Baltic a NATO lake and turns the Arctic into a new field of competition.
- [[MT184 - The War in Ukraine, Relations With China & Wider Geopolitics (With General David Petraeus)#^MT184-C09-X1|MT184-C09-X1]] — A World Economic Forum projection has China's population falling from 1.3 billion to little more than 600 million by 2100, a structural headwind raised in the question of whether Xi overestimates China's strength.
- [[MT184 - The War in Ukraine, Relations With China & Wider Geopolitics (With General David Petraeus)#^MT184-C11-1|MT184-C11-1]] — Regime change in Iran would show up as indicators like oil workers striking (as in 1979) or the security forces refusing to kill protesters (Egypt 2011); these are absent, so the regime probably is not overthrown soon, and Western support must be covert to avoid helping the regime.
- [[MT184 - The War in Ukraine, Relations With China & Wider Geopolitics (With General David Petraeus)#^MT184-C11-X1|MT184-C11-X1]] — Petraeus is more sceptical than Iran scholar Karim Sadjadpour, who now thinks the regime will fall: unarmed, leaderless protesters face an organised, armed Basij of over two million plus the Revolutionary Guards, so timing is uncertain.
- [[MT184 - The War in Ukraine, Relations With China & Wider Geopolitics (With General David Petraeus)#^MT184-C11-X3|MT184-C11-X3]] — Iran's playbook in Iraq and Syria is to replicate its Lebanon model: control powerful street militias plus a parliamentary bloc big enough to veto anything, giving influence without formal rule.
- [[MT184 - The War in Ukraine, Relations With China & Wider Geopolitics (With General David Petraeus)#^MT184-C12-1|MT184-C12-1]] — Freezing Russian reserves may cause tactical diversification (euro, pound, yuan) but no true displacement of the dollar, because no alternative offers rule of law, transparency and stability; even China did not unfreeze or break export controls.
- [[MT184 - The War in Ukraine, Relations With China & Wider Geopolitics (With General David Petraeus)#^MT184-C12-X1|MT184-C12-X1]] — Russia held roughly $600 billion of reserves abroad, about $200 billion of it in China, and all countries including China froze them, showing that rule-of-law dollar assets were treated as safe and that the Russia-China partnership had limits.
- [[MT184 - The War in Ukraine, Relations With China & Wider Geopolitics (With General David Petraeus)#^MT184-C12-X2|MT184-C12-X2]] — China exploits Russia's moment of weakness to obtain advantageous gas and oil deals while not breaching export controls in any meaningful way, a transactional partnership that has limits rather than being 'without limits'.
- [[MT184 - The War in Ukraine, Relations With China & Wider Geopolitics (With General David Petraeus)#^MT184-C13-X1|MT184-C13-X1]] — A proper zero-trust, managed cyber security solution takes roughly 85 discrete capabilities, so cyber security is a complex, multi-product spend; Petraeus sits on the board of the largest pure-play cyber services firm (caption: octave), owned by KKR, which also holds other cyber investments.
- [[MT184 - The War in Ukraine, Relations With China & Wider Geopolitics (With General David Petraeus)#^MT184-C14-1|MT184-C14-1]] — The world has moved from benign globalization where economics determined geopolitics to renewed great-power rivalry where geopolitics determines what is economically possible, constraining investment and trade.
- [[MT184 - The War in Ukraine, Relations With China & Wider Geopolitics (With General David Petraeus)#^MT184-C15-1|MT184-C15-1]] — Firms are moving from just-in-time to just-in-case logistics (higher inventories, warehouses) and diversifying manufacturing from China to Vietnam, India and Southeast Asia, which has capacity limits.
- [[MT184 - The War in Ukraine, Relations With China & Wider Geopolitics (With General David Petraeus)#^MT184-C16-1|MT184-C16-1]] — Rising Chinese labor costs and robotics lower the labor-cost gap so production can return to the US, Mexico or Canada, supporting reshoring.
- [[MT184 - The War in Ukraine, Relations With China & Wider Geopolitics (With General David Petraeus)#^MT184-C19-1|MT184-C19-1]] — The Cold War analogy fails because the US and China are each other's top-three trading partners; selective decoupling in critical technology is likely, not complete decoupling.

## [[MT185 - Toby Crabel on Vanishing Edges, AI Limits, and Future of Markets - Top Traders Unplugged -]]
- [[MT185 - Toby Crabel on Vanishing Edges, AI Limits, and Future of Markets - Top Traders Unplugged -#^MT185-C03-2|MT185-C03-2]] — Opening range breakout deteriorated (worst in decades in the last 2-3 years) because 24-hour trading removed the meaningful open as a reference point, a dissipation first seen in the 1980s with US Treasury night sessions.
- [[MT185 - Toby Crabel on Vanishing Edges, AI Limits, and Future of Markets - Top Traders Unplugged -#^MT185-C04-X1|MT185-C04-X1]] — Markets that still trade a defined primary session (the examples named are palm oil and Brazilian markets) show much better opening-range-breakout results, supporting the explanation that 24-hour trading destroyed the open as a reference point; in stock indices momentum is strong but crossover around an obscured open widens the path.
- [[MT185 - Toby Crabel on Vanishing Edges, AI Limits, and Future of Markets - Top Traders Unplugged -#^MT185-C07-X1|MT185-C07-X1]] — Candidate causes of edge decay over three to four decades include new products, more retail participation, prop shops, zero-DTE options and structured products; Crabel does not isolate one cause but detects change by looking at charts and then quantifying it.
- [[MT185 - Toby Crabel on Vanishing Edges, AI Limits, and Future of Markets - Top Traders Unplugged -#^MT185-C09-1|MT185-C09-1]] — Markets are news-prone and jumpy, so the short-term program's average hold has shortened from about 36 hours to less.
- [[MT185 - Toby Crabel on Vanishing Edges, AI Limits, and Future of Markets - Top Traders Unplugged -#^MT185-C09-4|MT185-C09-4]] — Markets are as tough as in 40-50 years; there is a '70s-like environment but with an unidentified further factor causing challenges.
- [[MT185 - Toby Crabel on Vanishing Edges, AI Limits, and Future of Markets - Top Traders Unplugged -#^MT185-C10-X1|MT185-C10-X1]] — AI coding tools (Codex and others) let staff build and program verbally, the CEO pushes a firm-wide mandate including back office, and the speaker sees this as the next major change, cutting development and operating cost.
- [[MT185 - Toby Crabel on Vanishing Edges, AI Limits, and Future of Markets - Top Traders Unplugged -#^MT185-C13-2|MT185-C13-2]] — The current environment resembles the 1970s (debasement, gold and silver, trend following profitable, large abrupt moves) but is bigger and global; the 200-day moving average has become more important.

## [[MT186 - Trading the Fragmented World of 2026 - Systematic Investor - Ep.382]]
- [[MT186 - Trading the Fragmented World of 2026 - Systematic Investor - Ep.382#^MT186-C02-3|MT186-C02-3]] — In a regionalized world release valves work slowly, so energy, inflation, currency and trade imbalances stay local and last longer, creating persistent trends.
- [[MT186 - Trading the Fragmented World of 2026 - Systematic Investor - Ep.382#^MT186-C03-1|MT186-C03-1]] — A fragmented world raises the risk of political shocks, sharp reversals and choppy periods for trend followers, though the underlying structure still favours persistence.
- [[MT186 - Trading the Fragmented World of 2026 - Systematic Investor - Ep.382#^MT186-C03-2|MT186-C03-2]] — Brennan judges the current fragmented-world period as the most favourable for trend following in years.
- [[MT186 - Trading the Fragmented World of 2026 - Systematic Investor - Ep.382#^MT186-C03-3|MT186-C03-3]] — Fights over resources drive commodity trends, and in 2025 trend following performance came from commodity markets rather than financial markets.

## [[MT187 - Treasury Markets not as “Safe” as You Think - Systematic Investor - Ep.414]]
- [[MT187 - Treasury Markets not as “Safe” as You Think - Systematic Investor - Ep.414#^MT187-C02-X1|MT187-C02-X1]] — The new listing of single-stock futures may draw more systematic futures managers into single-name trading, which exposes them to gap risk far beyond that of traditional futures markets.
- [[MT187 - Treasury Markets not as “Safe” as You Think - Systematic Investor - Ep.414#^MT187-C08-3|MT187-C08-3]] — Cash Treasuries trade OTC through primary dealers who also serve as auction conduits.
- [[MT187 - Treasury Markets not as “Safe” as You Think - Systematic Investor - Ep.414#^MT187-C14-3|MT187-C14-3]] — Research finds short-term trend following has weakened in practice.

## [[MT188 - Trend ETFs, CTA Replication, and the Fall of 60-40 - Systematic Investor - Ep.376]]
- [[MT188 - Trend ETFs, CTA Replication, and the Fall of 60-40 - Systematic Investor - Ep.376#^MT188-C02-1|MT188-C02-1]] — In this decade bonds stopped working as a diversifier, behaving like lousy equity exposure (lower returns, similar drawdown, higher volatility), pushing allocators to add a diversifying 'other' bucket.
- [[MT188 - Trend ETFs, CTA Replication, and the Fall of 60-40 - Systematic Investor - Ep.376#^MT188-C02-2|MT188-C02-2]] — Managed futures is a statistically obvious diversifier but is still zero in many portfolios; easy ETF access could make it standard.
- [[MT188 - Trend ETFs, CTA Replication, and the Fall of 60-40 - Systematic Investor - Ep.376#^MT188-C07-1|MT188-C07-1]] — Replication is an efficiency innovation to capture the trend signal, not a diluted version of CTA alpha; the industry largely disagrees.
- [[MT188 - Trend ETFs, CTA Replication, and the Fall of 60-40 - Systematic Investor - Ep.376#^MT188-C08-1|MT188-C08-1]] — Wealth managers lack a tradable index for CTA exposure, so an index around the replication strategy lets allocators model and benchmark it with long history (and via swaps).
- [[MT188 - Trend ETFs, CTA Replication, and the Fall of 60-40 - Systematic Investor - Ep.376#^MT188-C08-2|MT188-C08-2]] — The 2019 SEC derivatives rule made derivative-based ETFs easier, enabling trend and managed futures ETFs.
- [[MT188 - Trend ETFs, CTA Replication, and the Fall of 60-40 - Systematic Investor - Ep.376#^MT188-C09-1|MT188-C09-1]] — Simplify's index-based managed futures ETF charges 20bp plus 15bp acquired fund expenses; swap costs of accessing the index are not shown in the headline fee.
- [[MT188 - Trend ETFs, CTA Replication, and the Fall of 60-40 - Systematic Investor - Ep.376#^MT188-C11-1|MT188-C11-1]] — About 80% of US ETF assets cost below 20bp; ETF model portfolios hold only stocks and bonds, with zero diversifier exposure.
- [[MT188 - Trend ETFs, CTA Replication, and the Fall of 60-40 - Systematic Investor - Ep.376#^MT188-C12-X1|MT188-C12-X1]] — Accessing a strategy through swaps or derivatives on an index allows structuring flexibility (notes, principal protection, excess-return versions) that can improve tax efficiency, so tax effectiveness should be counted alongside fees when comparing implementations.

## [[MT189 - Trend Following Is Becoming Something Bigger Than Crisis Alpha - Systematic Investor - Ep]]
- [[MT189 - Trend Following Is Becoming Something Bigger Than Crisis Alpha - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT189-C07-1|MT189-C07-1]] — In fundamental commodity trading (e.g. natural gas with step-function costs from coal to gas) distributions are non-normal and information asymmetries are large.
- [[MT189 - Trend Following Is Becoming Something Bigger Than Crisis Alpha - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT189-C09-2|MT189-C09-2]] — SMA usage keeps growing, offering capital efficiency (margin only), cross-margining, liquidity, transparency and customisation.
- [[MT189 - Trend Following Is Becoming Something Bigger Than Crisis Alpha - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT189-C10-R0|MT189-C10-R0]] — Beer says investors differ widely in preferences (capital efficiency, customisation, vehicle), so different vehicles for one strategy serve different investors.
- [[MT189 - Trend Following Is Becoming Something Bigger Than Crisis Alpha - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT189-C11-1|MT189-C11-1]] — Over 25 years of extra engineering and instruments the CTA Sharpe ratio has not improved.
- [[MT189 - Trend Following Is Becoming Something Bigger Than Crisis Alpha - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT189-C11-X2|MT189-C11-X2]] — Before the early 2000s CTAs were a managed-account business for high-net-worth futures accounts (Dean Witter, John W. Henry, Campbell) with heavy leverage; the hedge-fund wrapper was a historical accident, and the later spread into mutual funds, UCITS, QIS, managed accounts and ETFs is a return to flexible delivery.
- [[MT189 - Trend Following Is Becoming Something Bigger Than Crisis Alpha - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT189-C12-1|MT189-C12-1]] — SG splits CTAs into trend (dominated by large blue-chip firms) and non-trend (short-term, quant macro, commodity specialists), with non-trend niche or blending into quant multi-strats.
- [[MT189 - Trend Following Is Becoming Something Bigger Than Crisis Alpha - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT189-C12-X2|MT189-C12-X2]] — Non-trend CTAs are blending into quant multi-strategy groups, and a growing set of sophisticated managed-account investors (multi-strats and new fund-group structures) are happy to allocate to high-Sharpe, low-capacity strategies that large trend programmes cannot offer.
- [[MT189 - Trend Following Is Becoming Something Bigger Than Crisis Alpha - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT189-C13-2|MT189-C13-2]] — Quant macro (fundamental forward-looking data) was expected to grow versus trend but many such groups closed.
- [[MT189 - Trend Following Is Becoming Something Bigger Than Crisis Alpha - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT189-C13-3|MT189-C13-3]] — Calling trend 'plain vanilla' invites cheap, undifferentiated products which then failed.
- [[MT189 - Trend Following Is Becoming Something Bigger Than Crisis Alpha - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT189-C13-R1|MT189-C13-R1]] — Kaastrup-Larsen restates his point: trend is not plain vanilla, but its role as a portfolio element is now more broadly accepted than ten years ago.
- [[MT189 - Trend Following Is Becoming Something Bigger Than Crisis Alpha - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT189-C14-X1|MT189-C14-X1]] — Persistent investment opportunities exist because smart participants face constraints ('handcuffs', such as fixed strategic asset allocations) that stop them acting on them, in the spirit of the economists-and-the-$100-bill joke; this is the reason to expect trend alpha to persist.
- [[MT189 - Trend Following Is Becoming Something Bigger Than Crisis Alpha - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT189-C20-R1|MT189-C20-R1]] — Beer sees non-trend strategies as satellites to the core trend driver and not as big drivers of performance over time.

## [[MT190 - Trend Following Is Changing Faster Than Ever - Systematic Investor - Ep.409]]
- [[MT190 - Trend Following Is Changing Faster Than Ever - Systematic Investor - Ep.409#^MT190-C01-1|MT190-C01-1]] — Practical issues such as tax treatment of dividends and price changes for single-stock futures (and Kalshi compute contracts) can block adoption even when the instruments are attractive.
- [[MT190 - Trend Following Is Changing Faster Than Ever - Systematic Investor - Ep.409#^MT190-C01-X1|MT190-C01-X1]] — Forward curves on the cost of AI compute power (priced off Nvidia chips) are a new commodity-like risk factor with no historical precedent, which a systematic manager can view as a diversifying exposure if its tax and operational treatment becomes clear.
- [[MT190 - Trend Following Is Changing Faster Than Ever - Systematic Investor - Ep.409#^MT190-C01-X2|MT190-C01-X2]] — Adoption of single-stock futures among CTAs is mixed: a minority strongly favour trading single names rather than only indices, while most CTAs still stick to index futures.
- [[MT190 - Trend Following Is Changing Faster Than Ever - Systematic Investor - Ep.409#^MT190-C02-1|MT190-C02-1]] — Single-stock futures make shorting easy because no borrow is needed, giving CTAs and others new diversifying risk factors.
- [[MT190 - Trend Following Is Changing Faster Than Ever - Systematic Investor - Ep.409#^MT190-C05-2|MT190-C05-2]] — Commodity contract months are different assets linked only by storage/inventories, so risk factors differ by maturity (answer to listener question on modelling).
- [[MT190 - Trend Following Is Changing Faster Than Ever - Systematic Investor - Ep.409#^MT190-C06-X1|MT190-C06-X1]] — In agricultural curves the front is driven by demand because the crop size is already largely known, while deferred contracts (new crop) are driven by supply risks such as weather and disease, so different maturities carry distinct risk factors and different diversification value.
- [[MT190 - Trend Following Is Changing Faster Than Ever - Systematic Investor - Ep.409#^MT190-C08-1|MT190-C08-1]] — CFM blog 'Seven Degrees of Market Structure': COVID-era retail inflows, API-based systematic retail, 0DTE options, rising dispersion and more mean reverting indices.
- [[MT190 - Trend Following Is Changing Faster Than Ever - Systematic Investor - Ep.409#^MT190-C08-2|MT190-C08-2]] — 0DTE index options exploded since weekly expiries in 2012, driven by retail selling at-the-money straddles to harvest the variance risk premium.
- [[MT190 - Trend Following Is Changing Faster Than Ever - Systematic Investor - Ep.409#^MT190-C08-3|MT190-C08-3]] — Implied correlation/dispersion index shows single-stock variance far above index variance, globally at an all-time high.
- [[MT190 - Trend Following Is Changing Faster Than Ever - Systematic Investor - Ep.409#^MT190-C08-4|MT190-C08-4]] — Buying dips was historically a mistake but over the last 10 years the index has become more mean reverting and dip-buying pays within a month.
- [[MT190 - Trend Following Is Changing Faster Than Ever - Systematic Investor - Ep.409#^MT190-C09-2|MT190-C09-2]] — The 0DTE gamma effect reduces index realised and implied volatility and has turned intraday and one-day equity autocorrelation negative; single stocks are less affected.
- [[MT190 - Trend Following Is Changing Faster Than Ever - Systematic Investor - Ep.409#^MT190-C10-1|MT190-C10-1]] — Suppressed index volatility makes single-stock futures a better source of diversified risk for trend followers.
- [[MT190 - Trend Following Is Changing Faster Than Ever - Systematic Investor - Ep.409#^MT190-C10-X1|MT190-C10-X1]] — Launching single-stock futures should not reduce single-stock dispersion because the cause is the index options explosion (0DTE), not the ease of shorting; single-stock options are less liquid and the CME contracts cover only about 50 very liquid names.
- [[MT190 - Trend Following Is Changing Faster Than Ever - Systematic Investor - Ep.409#^MT190-C10-X2|MT190-C10-X2]] — Short-dated and weekly option expiries are spreading from equity indices to bond futures and oil, so the options-gamma mechanism that suppresses equity index trends could appear in other markets, with implications for short-term trend following there.
- [[MT190 - Trend Following Is Changing Faster Than Ever - Systematic Investor - Ep.409#^MT190-C11-2|MT190-C11-2]] — The blog gives a mechanism (retail 0DTE selling) complementing the CFM order-book sparsity explanation of why short-term equity trend has declined.
- [[MT190 - Trend Following Is Changing Faster Than Ever - Systematic Investor - Ep.409#^MT190-C12-1|MT190-C12-1]] — A 1% fall in one-day autocorrelation costs a 1-day-signal trend follower about 0.16 Sharpe, but a 5/20-day crossover only about 3bp per market (about 6bp across equities), so a 3% fall costs about 0.2 Sharpe for fast traders and little for slower ones.
- [[MT190 - Trend Following Is Changing Faster Than Ever - Systematic Investor - Ep.409#^MT190-C12-3|MT190-C12-3]] — Toby Crabel observed that open-to-close follow-through fell from about 58% to about 53-54%, a significant headwind for short-term managers.
- [[MT190 - Trend Following Is Changing Faster Than Ever - Systematic Investor - Ep.409#^MT190-C12-X1|MT190-C12-X1]] — One-day autocorrelation in equity returns has fallen by about three percentage points over roughly 20 years, enough to cost fast equity trend followers about 0.2 Sharpe while leaving slower, macro-driven trend followers largely unaffected.
- [[MT190 - Trend Following Is Changing Faster Than Ever - Systematic Investor - Ep.409#^MT190-C13-1|MT190-C13-1]] — Index explains a shrinking share of single-stock variance (35% to 25-30%); industry plus Barra factors take it to about 50%; themes (news-driven, transient) explain about 5% more, leaving about 45% idiosyncratic.
- [[MT190 - Trend Following Is Changing Faster Than Ever - Systematic Investor - Ep.409#^MT190-C16-1|MT190-C16-1]] — AI and machine learning now process news instantly, so the news underreaction/narrative momentum edge is likely to disappear quickly.
- [[MT190 - Trend Following Is Changing Faster Than Ever - Systematic Investor - Ep.409#^MT190-C16-3|MT190-C16-3]] — Equity index futures cover only about 30% of harvestable equity risk; medium and long-term equity trend remains fine.
- [[MT190 - Trend Following Is Changing Faster Than Ever - Systematic Investor - Ep.409#^MT190-C17-X1|MT190-C17-X1]] — Narrative-based equity trend is harder than the other layers because the narrative covers only about 5% of variance and its identity keeps changing, so a manager has to track both the trend and which risk factor is currently live.

## [[MT191 - Trend Following Rewards Investors Who Wait - Systematic Investor - Ep.422]]
- [[MT191 - Trend Following Rewards Investors Who Wait - Systematic Investor - Ep.422#^MT191-C03-5|MT191-C03-5]] — In 2026 the MSCI World gained 12.45 percent but EAFE only 8.72 percent, showing the equity rally is narrow and driven by the US; the world government bond index was down about 3 percent.
- [[MT191 - Trend Following Rewards Investors Who Wait - Systematic Investor - Ep.422#^MT191-C03-X2|MT191-C03-X2]] — The S&P 500 was up 13.78 percent year to date in October 2026, ahead of MSCI World (12.45 percent) and EAFE (8.72 percent), which is the data behind the claim that the equity rally is narrow and US-led.
- [[MT191 - Trend Following Rewards Investors Who Wait - Systematic Investor - Ep.422#^MT191-C04-2|MT191-C04-2]] — Measured geopolitical risk has trended up recently with large shocks such as the US-Iran conflict.
- [[MT191 - Trend Following Rewards Investors Who Wait - Systematic Investor - Ep.422#^MT191-C05-2|MT191-C05-2]] — Geopolitical risk has real firm-level effects that vary by company, with companies exposed to international trade more affected.
- [[MT191 - Trend Following Rewards Investors Who Wait - Systematic Investor - Ep.422#^MT191-C06-1|MT191-C06-1]] — A 60/40 portfolio is well diversified in a low-inflation, low-geopolitical-risk world, but becomes vulnerable when geopolitical risk, deglobalisation and other threats are added.
- [[MT191 - Trend Following Rewards Investors Who Wait - Systematic Investor - Ep.422#^MT191-C08-3|MT191-C08-3]] — For most of trend's history short bond positions did not pay because of the secular bond bull market, so trend was accused of having a long-bond bias.
- [[MT191 - Trend Following Rewards Investors Who Wait - Systematic Investor - Ep.422#^MT191-C12-5|MT191-C12-5]] — Commodity indices are heavily weighted to energy, so index exposure is undiversified (e.g. little wheat exposure when wheat moved in summer 2026).

## [[MT192 - Trend Following Still Works — So Why Do Allocators Ignore It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.3]]
- [[MT192 - Trend Following Still Works — So Why Do Allocators Ignore It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.3#^MT192-C08-5|MT192-C08-5]] — Bonds' diversification benefit was boosted by falling interest rates through 2021, so the result may not persist.
- [[MT192 - Trend Following Still Works — So Why Do Allocators Ignore It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.3#^MT192-C09-4|MT192-C09-4]] — The 60/40 portfolio had a Sharpe of about 0.76 since 1990 but only about 0.55 since 2000.
- [[MT192 - Trend Following Still Works — So Why Do Allocators Ignore It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.3#^MT192-C10-2|MT192-C10-2]] — The CTA industry has become slower: the correlation of the simple indicator run at slower speeds with the SG index rose over 15 years.
- [[MT192 - Trend Following Still Works — So Why Do Allocators Ignore It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.3#^MT192-C12-X1|MT192-C12-X1]] — Before the 2011-12 time-series momentum paper, trend managers kept their methods secret because a simple, explainable rule looks trivial and clients would then refuse to pay fees for it; transparency later made trend commonplace and harder to charge for.

## [[MT193 - Trend Following as a Return Enhancement Overlay - Systematic Investor 297]]
- [[MT193 - Trend Following as a Return Enhancement Overlay - Systematic Investor 297#^MT193-C04-1|MT193-C04-1]] — Decline of the neoliberal order (free trade, globalization) has driven nationalism and gains on the far left and far right, relevant for European elections.
- [[MT193 - Trend Following as a Return Enhancement Overlay - Systematic Investor 297#^MT193-C08-X2|MT193-C08-X2]] — More managers have launched dedicated systematic or quant macro programs in recent years (price- or economic-data inputs, directional or relative value), making quant macro a distinct universe worth monitoring as a diversifier next to trend.
- [[MT193 - Trend Following as a Return Enhancement Overlay - Systematic Investor 297#^MT193-C10-2|MT193-C10-2]] — The UBS 2024 survey of 320 single family offices (average net worth 2.6 billion) shows 28 percent public equity, 22 percent private equity, 19 percent fixed income, 10 percent real estate, 5 percent hedge funds, private debt 2 percent.
- [[MT193 - Trend Following as a Return Enhancement Overlay - Systematic Investor 297#^MT193-C11-2|MT193-C11-2]] — Long Angle member survey: about 60 percent public and private equity, 35 percent alternatives and 10 percent cash and fixed income.

## [[MT194 - Trend Following during Trump Tariff Turmoil! - Systematic Investor 344]]
- [[MT194 - Trend Following during Trump Tariff Turmoil! - Systematic Investor 344#^MT194-C03-5|MT194-C03-5]] — In the tariff shock equities fell while yields rose and the dollar fell, unlike the usual safe-haven response, indicating vulnerability in US assets and a possibly new regime for bonds and the dollar.
- [[MT194 - Trend Following during Trump Tariff Turmoil! - Systematic Investor 344#^MT194-C04-2|MT194-C04-2]] — Tariff policy aimed at fixing trade imbalances should have been dollar-positive but instead the dollar lost ground, raising questions about its key-currency role.
- [[MT194 - Trend Following during Trump Tariff Turmoil! - Systematic Investor 344#^MT194-C12-3|MT194-C12-3]] — The trend industry has consistently shifted to slower systems, reflecting trading costs and the capture of long-term risk premia, which worked until recently.
- [[MT194 - Trend Following during Trump Tariff Turmoil! - Systematic Investor 344#^MT194-C17-1|MT194-C17-1]] — After 2020 fixed income became vulnerable (inflation, US assets), so correlations matter more and must be incorporated, with skepticism about their volatility.

## [[MT195 - Trend Following in 2025- New Challenges, New Strategies! - Systematic Investor 326]]
- [[MT195 - Trend Following in 2025- New Challenges, New Strategies! - Systematic Investor 326#^MT195-C05-2|MT195-C05-2]] — Regulatory changes in the US made active ETFs viable, supporting the managed futures ETF wave including BlackRock's filing.
- [[MT195 - Trend Following in 2025- New Challenges, New Strategies! - Systematic Investor 326#^MT195-C05-3|MT195-C05-3]] — New product spaces go through proliferation, innovation and consolidation, and maturation phases; managed futures ETFs are in proliferation.
- [[MT195 - Trend Following in 2025- New Challenges, New Strategies! - Systematic Investor 326#^MT195-C05-X1|MT195-C05-X1]] — A trend program that trades a small universe (around 20 markets) is structurally tilted to large markets such as the S&P 500, so its result in a year like 2024 depends on how many markets it trades; it is effectively a different strategy from a broad one.
- [[MT195 - Trend Following in 2025- New Challenges, New Strategies! - Systematic Investor 326#^MT195-C05-X2|MT195-C05-X2]] — BlackRock's managed futures ETF filing signals that managed futures could become a viable allocation inside the disaggregated US pension system, where few people have had access to it.
- [[MT195 - Trend Following in 2025- New Challenges, New Strategies! - Systematic Investor 326#^MT195-C12-2|MT195-C12-2]] — Trend positions aggregate into a macro-themed exposure built purely from price moves.

## [[MT196 - Trend Following in Crisis- - Systematic Investor 346]]
- [[MT196 - Trend Following in Crisis- - Systematic Investor 346#^MT196-C02-3|MT196-C02-3]] — Bonds' safe-haven reputation may be recency bias from a 40-year falling-rate cycle, and may not hold if rates trend higher.
- [[MT196 - Trend Following in Crisis- - Systematic Investor 346#^MT196-C03-2|MT196-C03-2]] — Breakdown of institutional rules and frameworks is bad for US assets and markets.
- [[MT196 - Trend Following in Crisis- - Systematic Investor 346#^MT196-C16-3|MT196-C16-3]] — Trend followers form a family tree with distinct lineages (US pit-trading wing, British quantitative wing, Scandinavian), which explains differences of opinion about sizing and position changes.
- [[MT196 - Trend Following in Crisis- - Systematic Investor 346#^MT196-C17-X1|MT196-C17-X1]] — After a long stretch of weak trend returns, most trend-following firms diversified into other systematic strategies, either inside the flagship fund or as separate funds, which leaves open the question whether pure trend still works.
- [[MT196 - Trend Following in Crisis- - Systematic Investor 346#^MT196-C18-2|MT196-C18-2]] — Winton has rebuilt its trend-following allocation and again manages a lot of money there.

## [[MT197 - Trend Following over the next few years - Systematic Investor 314]]
- [[MT197 - Trend Following over the next few years - Systematic Investor 314#^MT197-C01-1|MT197-C01-1]] — Replication, already done for hedge fund strategies including managed futures, is being attempted for private equity indices to create a liquid version of an illiquid asset, which raises many quant and replication problems.
- [[MT197 - Trend Following over the next few years - Systematic Investor 314#^MT197-C07-3|MT197-C07-3]] — The one-million-job downward employment revision was unknowable, so the response is to follow prices.
- [[MT197 - Trend Following over the next few years - Systematic Investor 314#^MT197-C14-1|MT197-C14-1]] — Markets trade in clusters (base metals driven by business cycle, precious metals, energy) with spillovers to the real economy.

## [[MT198 - Trend Following to Super Determinism - Systematic Investor 342]]
- [[MT198 - Trend Following to Super Determinism - Systematic Investor 342#^MT198-C02-X1|MT198-C02-X1]] — Dunn Capital, founded about 50 years ago and now in its 51st year, is arguably the longest-running pure trend follower because other pioneers of the same era (e.g. Campbell, Millburn) broadened into other strategies; a half-century live record of one trend approach is evidence of its robustness.
- [[MT198 - Trend Following to Super Determinism - Systematic Investor 342#^MT198-C03-1|MT198-C03-1]] — For 20 years markets were driven by central bank guidance, but now fiscal policy (US deficit cut from 6-7% to 3%, Europe expanding deficits from 2-3% to 5-6%) and geopolitics may matter more than a 25bp difference in interest rates.
- [[MT198 - Trend Following to Super Determinism - Systematic Investor 342#^MT198-C14-X1|MT198-C14-X1]] — The technology and internet revolution removed borders for data and communication, so agents everywhere now influence each other and the complexity and interconnectedness of the market system has compounded (and will increase with virtual reality and AI), unlike 30 years ago.

## [[MT199 - Trend Following too complicated- - Systematic Investor 296]]
- [[MT199 - Trend Following too complicated- - Systematic Investor 296#^MT199-C02-1|MT199-C02-1]] — Market plumbing (clearing, transparent rules) matters; futures markets are transparent and need minimal regulation for liquidity, unlike crypto where opaque money movement creates arbitrage and structural risks.
- [[MT199 - Trend Following too complicated- - Systematic Investor 296#^MT199-C03-1|MT199-C03-1]] — Per the SG Capital Consulting survey (472 responses, 315 firms), the largest rise in investor interest vs fall 2023 was fixed income relative value, linked to high rate volatility (MOVE index) and a higher-rate regime with more dispersion.
- [[MT199 - Trend Following too complicated- - Systematic Investor 296#^MT199-C04-2|MT199-C04-2]] — Investors seek discretionary global macro (interest about 50% in the survey throughout) which is hard to find; quantitative global macro is less liked; CTA trend sits in between as a substitute with convexity and global exposure, a 'poor man's global macro'.
- [[MT199 - Trend Following too complicated- - Systematic Investor 296#^MT199-C06-2|MT199-C06-2]] — Chinese equity growth has not translated into stock market wealth creation versus India, and the 2024 Chinese rally followed government and state entity buying and changed perceptions.
- [[MT199 - Trend Following too complicated- - Systematic Investor 296#^MT199-C07-1|MT199-C07-1]] — The liberal order (free trade, free capital flow, WTO) is eroding via sanctions, tariffs (continued by Biden) and capital controls, moving from multilateralism to bilateralism, causing more price dispersion, dislocation, uncertainty, lower cross-market correlation and more tail risk, so convexity and global macro are favoured.
- [[MT199 - Trend Following too complicated- - Systematic Investor 296#^MT199-C08-1|MT199-C08-1]] — In the age of disorder, equity indices across countries correlate less, creating more opportunities to trade countries separately.
- [[MT199 - Trend Following too complicated- - Systematic Investor 296#^MT199-C08-X1|MT199-C08-X1]] — Differences in correlation between country equity indices partly reflect sector composition (FTSE energy-heavy, other markets tech-heavy), so lower cross-country correlation is not purely a deglobalisation effect and will persist for structural reasons.
- [[MT199 - Trend Following too complicated- - Systematic Investor 296#^MT199-C09-2|MT199-C09-2]] — If the world becomes more disordered, price trends may last longer; tariffs and trade barriers prevent price equilibration so inflation dispersion across countries and trends persist.

## [[MT200 - Trend Following- How much to Allocate- - Systematic Investor 316]]
- [[MT200 - Trend Following- How much to Allocate- - Systematic Investor 316#^MT200-C14-1|MT200-C14-1]] — Roy Niedhoefer's 2014 paper argued trend followers would struggle when rates rise; instead shorting bonds became profitable, showing no one knows the future.
- [[MT200 - Trend Following- How much to Allocate- - Systematic Investor 316#^MT200-C16-2|MT200-C16-2]] — Include trend following inside strategic asset allocation (5-20%) so the benchmark absorbs the tracking error.
- [[MT200 - Trend Following- How much to Allocate- - Systematic Investor 316#^MT200-C17-R1|MT200-C17-R1]] — Baltas says his 2019 sizing paper became relevant again for many clients because asset owners are asking more questions about 60/40 and trend than five years ago.

## [[MT201 - Trend Following...Austrian Style - Open Interest 8]]
- [[MT201 - Trend Following...Austrian Style - Open Interest 8#^MT201-C05-2|MT201-C05-2]] — Over the last 30-40 years long trades in equity and bond trend following beat short trades, while currencies and commodities show a different pattern; this is a sample-specific effect of secular bull markets.
- [[MT201 - Trend Following...Austrian Style - Open Interest 8#^MT201-C07-X1|MT201-C07-X1]] — Alpha in small alternative markets is capacity-limited: a multi-billion fund can trade them but the allocation is too small to move performance, so only a mid-sized CTA like SMN can use them as a real source of return.
- [[MT201 - Trend Following...Austrian Style - Open Interest 8#^MT201-C08-2|MT201-C08-2]] — After stringent cost assumptions, adding less liquid markets still raises the portfolio Sharpe ratio significantly.
- [[MT201 - Trend Following...Austrian Style - Open Interest 8#^MT201-C10-X1|MT201-C10-X1]] — Volatility is a non-directional asset class, so SMN argues it needs its own signal design rather than the plain price-momentum rule used for the directional markets.
- [[MT201 - Trend Following...Austrian Style - Open Interest 8#^MT201-C11-1|MT201-C11-1]] — Trend following is unlikely to be arbitraged away because large outliers have causes other than CTA trading, though more noise could create more false signals.

## [[MT202 - Trump 2.0 & The Unpinning of Volatility - Systematic Investor 307]]
- [[MT202 - Trump 2.0 & The Unpinning of Volatility - Systematic Investor 307#^MT202-C04-5|MT202-C04-5]] — Volatility is not an asset class but the underlying distribution of all asset classes, so vol-market dynamics drive broad market performance.
- [[MT202 - Trump 2.0 & The Unpinning of Volatility - Systematic Investor 307#^MT202-C08-3|MT202-C08-3]] — Open borders were a release valve for US labour-market inflation; closing them is expected to accelerate labour inflation.
- [[MT202 - Trump 2.0 & The Unpinning of Volatility - Systematic Investor 307#^MT202-C08-X1|MT202-C08-X1]] — One of two release valves that kept US inflation down was the dollar: the US could manage the dollar and export inflation abroad; the second valve was open borders easing labour pressure, and both are threatened by tariffs and immigration limits.
- [[MT202 - Trump 2.0 & The Unpinning of Volatility - Systematic Investor 307#^MT202-C10-1|MT202-C10-1]] — In the short term US power plays (tariffs, activist Fed) benefit the US relative to the world, but over time they degrade trust in the system, a positive-short-term convex-tail outcome.
- [[MT202 - Trump 2.0 & The Unpinning of Volatility - Systematic Investor 307#^MT202-C13-1|MT202-C13-1]] — Protectionism and America First raise geopolitical conflict, oil shocks and fiscal spending, so more oil and copper supply control is expected, a supporting factor for commodities.

## [[MT203 - Trump's NEW Treasury Secretary on Macro Strategy ft. Scott Bessent]]
- [[MT203 - Trump's NEW Treasury Secretary on Macro Strategy ft. Scott Bessent#^MT203-C04-3|MT203-C04-3]] — Fixed exchange arrangements between mobile capital and structurally different economies are flawed and eventually break.
- [[MT203 - Trump's NEW Treasury Secretary on Macro Strategy ft. Scott Bessent#^MT203-C04-X2|MT203-C04-X2]] — The US absorbs regional shocks (1980s Texas energy bust) through labour mobility and federal fiscal transfers; the ERM/euro area had mobile capital but neither, which is why the currency arrangement was structurally flawed.
- [[MT203 - Trump's NEW Treasury Secretary on Macro Strategy ft. Scott Bessent#^MT203-C07-1|MT203-C07-1]] — China moving up the value chain exports deflation and threatens the business models of Korea and Germany.
- [[MT203 - Trump's NEW Treasury Secretary on Macro Strategy ft. Scott Bessent#^MT203-C08-5|MT203-C08-5]] — Gold and the S&P 500 have had similar price appreciation since 1967 (gold ahead since 2000 excluding dividends).
- [[MT203 - Trump's NEW Treasury Secretary on Macro Strategy ft. Scott Bessent#^MT203-C08-6|MT203-C08-6]] — Gold thesis is a long-term tectonic move from A to B with oscillations; the investor's skill is sizing up at the right times.
- [[MT203 - Trump's NEW Treasury Secretary on Macro Strategy ft. Scott Bessent#^MT203-C10-6|MT203-C10-6]] — Japanese equities benefit from replating and a corporate governance catch-up; the Nikkei exceeded its 1989 high.
- [[MT203 - Trump's NEW Treasury Secretary on Macro Strategy ft. Scott Bessent#^MT203-C10-X1|MT203-C10-X1]] — In 2011 consultants dismissed Abe's plan to end Japan's deflation because of demographics and debt, yet reflation arrived within ten years: another case where 'could never happen' marked an asymmetric opportunity.

## [[MT204 - Unpacking QIS- Structure, Scale, and Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.372]]
- [[MT204 - Unpacking QIS- Structure, Scale, and Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.372#^MT204-C04-3|MT204-C04-3]] — Alborne's H1 2025 survey puts QIS notional assets at about $1.3 trillion, split roughly 50/50 between bank and asset-manager QIS.
- [[MT204 - Unpacking QIS- Structure, Scale, and Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.372#^MT204-C04-4|MT204-C04-4]] — QIS assets are over half equity, then multi-asset and commodities; fixed income (about $50bn), FX ($27bn) and credit ($13bn) are small relative to their market size.
- [[MT204 - Unpacking QIS- Structure, Scale, and Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.372#^MT204-C05-2|MT204-C05-2]] — Broker-dealer QIS is about $600bn plus or minus $100bn on a rough estimate; the market grew with winter periods around 2018 and 2021.
- [[MT204 - Unpacking QIS- Structure, Scale, and Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.372#^MT204-C05-3|MT204-C05-3]] — On the asset-manager side QIS is mainly equity long-only smart beta, while bank QIS is more evenly split and multi-asset/cross-asset carry baskets are growing.
- [[MT204 - Unpacking QIS- Structure, Scale, and Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.372#^MT204-C05-X1|MT204-C05-X1]] — Fixed income and FX are a small part of QIS because banks' QIS business grew out of equity long/short, commodity roll indices and delta-1 products, and academic risk-premia work has been mostly on equities; Nick sees fixed income and FX as now in a growth phase.
- [[MT204 - Unpacking QIS- Structure, Scale, and Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.372#^MT204-C07-1|MT204-C07-1]] — QIS began with commodity roll indices and post-GFC factor investing by Nordic pensions that replaced asset-class mixes with factor mixes (equity market neutral, vol carry).
- [[MT204 - Unpacking QIS- Structure, Scale, and Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.372#^MT204-C07-X1|MT204-C07-X1]] — QIS grew not because clients suddenly believed in systematic investing but because an index or swap wrapper delivers a rules-based exposure with less operational burden and better cash efficiency than building it in-house.
- [[MT204 - Unpacking QIS- Structure, Scale, and Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.372#^MT204-C08-1|MT204-C08-1]] — Hedge funds buy intraday momentum and vol strategies from banks through QIS because the operational and technology burden is high.
- [[MT204 - Unpacking QIS- Structure, Scale, and Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.372#^MT204-C09-2|MT204-C09-2]] — Most QIS volume is delta-1 swap exposure; optionalised wrappers (call options capping losses) are a small share, rising in the last two to three years mainly from insurers.
- [[MT204 - Unpacking QIS- Structure, Scale, and Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.372#^MT204-C11-X1|MT204-C11-X1]] — Because QIS profitability depends on scale, popular bank trend/CTA indices cover only the deepest liquid markets (the host notes they omit smaller markets); broader indices with illiquid assets exist but support only a fraction of the capacity.

## [[MT205 - Unpacking the Chaos- What Today’s Market Signals Really Mean - Systematic Investor 343]]
- [[MT205 - Unpacking the Chaos- What Today’s Market Signals Really Mean - Systematic Investor 343#^MT205-C02-2|MT205-C02-2]] — Reliance on negative stock-bond correlation in crises is unsafe; 2022 broke it and the first two decades of the century were the anomaly.
- [[MT205 - Unpacking the Chaos- What Today’s Market Signals Really Mean - Systematic Investor 343#^MT205-C03-2|MT205-C03-2]] — When inflation is the concern, stocks and bonds are positively correlated, so a 60/40 portfolio cannot rely on bonds as a diversifier.
- [[MT205 - Unpacking the Chaos- What Today’s Market Signals Really Mean - Systematic Investor 343#^MT205-C03-4|MT205-C03-4]] — When one factor (inflation) drives commodities, equities and bonds together, diversification needs strategies like CTAs rather than traditional assets.
- [[MT205 - Unpacking the Chaos- What Today’s Market Signals Really Mean - Systematic Investor 343#^MT205-C07-4|MT205-C07-4]] — The CTA industry moved from faster trading in the early 2000s to 1-3 month trend speeds, so the SG CTA index reacts more slowly to rapid crashes and still held residual equity longs in 2025.
- [[MT205 - Unpacking the Chaos- What Today’s Market Signals Really Mean - Systematic Investor 343#^MT205-C14-X1|MT205-C14-X1]] — Because CTAs are about 70% loaded on the SG CTA index and about 50% correlated to each other, investors who are used to diversifying across macro managers see CTAs as interchangeable, which hurts the case for allocating to several of them.

## [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315]]
- [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315#^MT206-C07-1|MT206-C07-1]] — The failures of the mean reversion trade (Russia, Iran, arguably Japan) share a rule-of-law/property-rights problem that stops investors returning regardless of valuation.
- [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315#^MT206-C07-2|MT206-C07-2]] — The US-China bifurcation means China may suffer from the same structural investability problem, so cheap valuation alone will not close the gap with the West.
- [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315#^MT206-C08-3|MT206-C08-3]] — Inflation has a cyclical component (business cycle) and a secular component (policy regime), and for 40 years only the cyclical one mattered.
- [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315#^MT206-C09-1|MT206-C09-1]] — The 40-year deflationary era came from easy money flowing to the top, globalization, technology and low rates, and it produced record corporate margins and inequality.
- [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315#^MT206-C09-2|MT206-C09-2]] — Inequality gives populist politics that favor fiscal demand-push, redistribution, deglobalization, conflict and resource hoarding, a secular inflation regime like the 1970s.
- [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315#^MT206-C09-X1|MT206-C09-X1]] — Near-zero rates let companies fund projects with payoffs 20 years out (Amazon, Uber, Tesla), so low rates fed the technology boom that was a deflationary force.
- [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315#^MT206-C09-X2|MT206-C09-X2]] — As millennials and Gen Z gain political weight, voters prioritise median outcomes over aggregate growth, shifting policy toward redistribution, fiscal demand support and protection, which ends the deflationary regime.
- [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315#^MT206-C10-4|MT206-C10-4]] — The Fed will be forced back into a box of slow growth and structural inflation, a decade of stagflation, which is harder to manage than deflationary pressure.
- [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315#^MT206-C11-X1|MT206-C11-X1]] — The populist, structurally inflationary regime is expected to last 10-15 years but to arrive in fits and starts, not in a smooth line.
- [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315#^MT206-C12-1|MT206-C12-1]] — Structural inflation is driven by fiscal policy, global conflict, labor costs/protectionism and commodity prices, so Cham assesses candidates on these.
- [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315#^MT206-C13-2|MT206-C13-2]] — Cham puts about a 50% chance on Chinese action against Taiwan within four years and links it to Russia and Iran as one structured movement.
- [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315#^MT206-C13-X1|MT206-C13-X1]] — Trump's stated position that Taiwan must pay for US weapons signals less deterrence, which gives China an incentive to move sooner; a global holding pattern waits on the election result.
- [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315#^MT206-C14-1|MT206-C14-1]] — Under Trump China's move would come sooner and resolve sooner, possibly leading to a detente and a rally in Chinese assets; under Harris it is later and longer.
- [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315#^MT206-C14-2|MT206-C14-2]] — A Saudi deal under Trump could offset energy-driven inflation from stimulus, while rising energy costs hurt Europe and Japan.
- [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315#^MT206-C14-3|MT206-C14-3]] — Strong immigration has held down US labor inflation, so hardline restriction would be structurally inflationary.
- [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315#^MT206-C14-X1|MT206-C14-X1]] — A US-China detente is the main thing that could reverse part of the populist inflation regime, which is why the election outcome matters for the inflation outlook.
- [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315#^MT206-C15-1|MT206-C15-1]] — The House has never swung without the presidential candidate winning and Democrats can only hold the Senate with a Harris VP tie-break, so a Trump win means a united government and bigger moves.
- [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315#^MT206-C16-1|MT206-C16-1]] — Inflationary populist regimes hurt both equities and bonds: the last 14-year period of rising rates lost about 70% in real terms, so 60/40 suffers.
- [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315#^MT206-C16-X1|MT206-C16-X1]] — In rising-rate secular-inflation regimes equities de-rate because bonds offer an alternative and margins compress, while long bonds lose from higher rates, so both legs of 60/40 suffer.

## [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364]]
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C06-1|MT207-C06-1]] — Regional carbon markets (California, EU, RGGI) are mutually uncorrelated while EUA and UKA moved together after relinking talk, so each adds diversification.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C07-1|MT207-C07-1]] — Tariff news decoupled COMEX and LME copper, so the same commodity can behave differently by venue.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C14-3|MT207-C14-3]] — Rolling three-year annualized volatility of the Barclay CTA index fell from 20-25% to below 5%, due to institutionalization and asset gathering.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C17-2|MT207-C17-2]] — Fees did not fall in line with lower vol; multistrategy fees have risen, and some funds sit in single-digit returns.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C17-3|MT207-C17-3]] — Flat fees and ETF replicators below 1% show fee pressure in CTAs.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C19-1|MT207-C19-1]] — Trend following may be commoditized because rules are known, but its return stream is not beta.

## [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro]]
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C01-1|MT208-C01-1]] — The 2022 Fed tightening is not an ordinary rate cycle: it coincides with a pandemic recovery, a bursting global sovereign debt bubble, peak cheap energy and metals, and a US-China cold war, so old playbooks fail.
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C01-3|MT208-C01-3]] — Peak cheap energy and metals (falling ore grades) means higher prices are needed for supply, creating a persistent commodity inflation impulse.
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C01-4|MT208-C01-4]] — Chinese water constraints become a power shortage, which hits the world's factory and exports inflation.
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C02-1|MT208-C02-1]] — China is a different adversary from the USSR: GDP above 60% of US since 2014 and the US offshored manufacturing to it, so trade and supply-chain frictions will be stickier than expected.
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C02-2|MT208-C02-2]] — Technology deflation conflicts with a debt-backed system that needs inflation (Jeff Booth), a two-horses-one-ass tension that will force policy choices.
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C03-X1|MT208-C03-X1]] — Host argues pandemic policies broke supply chains first and that pushed prices up; Gromen says the order does not matter: running demand as hot as the stimulus did overloaded stretched supply chains, so demand-led deleveraging by inflation is capped by supply capacity.
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C10-1|MT208-C10-1]] — China's northern aquifers are being drained far faster than they replenish (tens of thousands of years), so the constraint is structural not weather-driven.
