- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C10-2|MT208-C10-2]] — Evidence of power rationing: 3Q21 plant shutdowns, Apple factory shutdowns in 4Q21 and Bitcoin mining migration, which Gopal Reddy ties to water-driven power scarcity.
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C10-X1|MT208-C10-X1]] — Because China always prioritizes growth over the environment, power-plant shutdowns in 3Q21 presented as environmental are read as evidence of a real power shortage; Bitcoin miners are the first load to be shed when electricity is scarce.
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C11-1|MT208-C11-1]] — Water scarcity cuts hydro base-load and raises water-intensive coal and nuclear needs, so it links directly to China's output.
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C11-2|MT208-C11-2]] — The water-power problem could cause major issues within a couple of years, possibly late 2022, but good rain could delay it.
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C11-3|MT208-C11-3]] — Consensus that supply-chain disruption has peaked is wrong because the China water problem makes shortages endemic.
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C11-X1|MT208-C11-X1]] — In December 2021 Shenzhen and Guangzhou, the main tech manufacturing region, asked citizens to ration water, which Gromen cites as a further 'shark sighting' of acute water stress.
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C16-4|MT208-C16-4]] — If supply constraints are structural, CPI could run 7-10% for 2-4 years, a very different case from cyclical recovery inflation.
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C17-1|MT208-C17-1]] — ESG and climate-change initiatives add inflation.
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C17-2|MT208-C17-2]] — Saudi Arabia will eventually concede oil sales to China in yuan because China is its largest customer.
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C17-3|MT208-C17-3]] — Yuan-priced oil sales would remove dollar demand from oil buying, while the US keeps issuing deficits, so structurally the dollar weakens (Saudi riyal might fall, lowering oil costs in the short run).
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C17-X2|MT208-C17-X2]] — Gromen cites credible rumours of a mid-2020 China-Saudi deal for some oil to be paid in yuan or dollars at China's choice, unconfirmed, with GCC ministers visiting China, so the move away from dollar oil pricing is probably under way and the open question is disclosure.
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C18-X1|MT208-C18-X1]] — A yuan oil deal is structurally negative for the dollar (less foreign demand, the Fed must absorb issuance) but could be dollar-positive and oil-negative in the short run, so market impact is uncertain.
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C20-1|MT208-C20-1]] — Bill Miller's 50% Bitcoin / 50% Amazon portfolio and Dalio's 2% Bitcoin signal that senior investors expect the Fed to be trapped.

## [[MT209 - What is the fiscal rule-]]
- [[MT209 - What is the fiscal rule-#^MT209-C01-4|MT209-C01-4]] — The fund grew far beyond expectations through higher production, higher prices and good investment management, which made the fiscal rule more important.

## [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390]]
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C02-X1|MT210-C02-X1]] — European equities reacted more than US ones to the Iran shock, arguably because European gas prices responded even more, so the energy channel (gas) explains regional equity differences.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C05-4|MT210-C05-4]] — Source 2: long and short positions in commodities and currencies, which captured the 2007 oil run-up, the 2008 sell-off and 2014-15 volatility.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C06-6|MT210-C06-6]] — In Niels's data equities are not a strong sector for trend following, while bonds (the 40 of 60/40) have been good.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C08-2|MT210-C08-2]] — Total portfolio approach (TPA) may lead to larger allocations to pure diversifiers like trend.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C09-1|MT210-C09-1]] — Sovereign wealth funds using TPA see trend's portfolio benefit clearly, which should boost allocations.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C10-3|MT210-C10-3]] — The Citrini report is an outlier scenario of AI-driven white-collar unemployment and eroded moats, not a forecast.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C11-4|MT210-C11-4]] — Productivity gains may go to capital owners rather than wages (Gilded Age: benign deflation, real wage gains lagged).
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C12-3|MT210-C12-3]] — AI can ease aggregate supply constraints but leaves labor-market mismatches (white-collar to health care), so imbalances persist.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C12-X1|MT210-C12-X1]] — Expectations for AI's productivity effect moved in a year from a debate over 0.1% versus 0.5% to a much bigger expected impact, yet there is no consensus on the policy response, which is where the macro risk sits.

## [[MT211 - When Prices Stop Making Sense - Systematic Investor - Ep.378]]
- [[MT211 - When Prices Stop Making Sense - Systematic Investor - Ep.378#^MT211-C03-1|MT211-C03-1]] — The Fed's projections of higher growth without lower unemployment imply it is buying the AI productivity story.
- [[MT211 - When Prices Stop Making Sense - Systematic Investor - Ep.378#^MT211-C09-2|MT211-C09-2]] — Gold may be in a regime change because central banks are buying, safe-asset supply from Treasuries is falling, and retail abroad is buying on government uncertainty.
- [[MT211 - When Prices Stop Making Sense - Systematic Investor - Ep.378#^MT211-C13-1|MT211-C13-1]] — Tariff-driven divergence between COMEX and LME copper creates ambiguity about which price to use to generate trend signals.

## [[MT212 - Why Cooperation Is Essential in Crisis - IMF Managing Director Kristalina Georgieva]]
- [[MT212 - Why Cooperation Is Essential in Crisis - IMF Managing Director Kristalina Georgieva#^MT212-C02-2|MT212-C02-2]] — The world economy's resilience has not been fully tested against geopolitics, technology, demography and climate together.
- [[MT212 - Why Cooperation Is Essential in Crisis - IMF Managing Director Kristalina Georgieva#^MT212-C02-X1|MT212-C02-X1]] — The IMF argues the lasting benefit comes from good policies and responsible institutions that let a country grow on its own after the emergency cushion, rather than from repeated bailouts.
- [[MT212 - Why Cooperation Is Essential in Crisis - IMF Managing Director Kristalina Georgieva#^MT212-C03-1|MT212-C03-1]] — Reason one for resilience: since the financial crisis countries have independent central banks, fiscal rules and better tax systems, which cushion shocks.
- [[MT212 - Why Cooperation Is Essential in Crisis - IMF Managing Director Kristalina Georgieva#^MT212-C03-2|MT212-C03-2]] — Reason two: a more agile private sector adapts to shocks, e.g. front-loading imports before tariffs.
- [[MT212 - Why Cooperation Is Essential in Crisis - IMF Managing Director Kristalina Georgieva#^MT212-C04-4|MT212-C04-4]] — The IMF AI Preparedness Index (digital infrastructure, labor flexibility/skills, innovation, regulation/ethics) shows advanced economies (Singapore, Denmark, US) ready and most developing countries not.
- [[MT212 - Why Cooperation Is Essential in Crisis - IMF Managing Director Kristalina Georgieva#^MT212-C04-5|MT212-C04-5]] — The IMF estimates AI could add 0.1 to 0.8 percentage points of extra global growth, a large amount given slow growth.
- [[MT212 - Why Cooperation Is Essential in Crisis - IMF Managing Director Kristalina Georgieva#^MT212-C04-6|MT212-C04-6]] — About 60% of jobs in rich countries, 40% globally and 26% in low-income countries will be affected by AI (enhanced, changed or eliminated), a labor-market tsunami.
- [[MT212 - Why Cooperation Is Essential in Crisis - IMF Managing Director Kristalina Georgieva#^MT212-C04-X2|MT212-C04-X2]] — Climate-related financial stability risk has a transition channel (rapid economic change) and a physical channel (shocks to businesses and households); the transition channel looks smaller now because decarbonization is proceeding more slowly.
- [[MT212 - Why Cooperation Is Essential in Crisis - IMF Managing Director Kristalina Georgieva#^MT212-C05-1|MT212-C05-1]] — AI risks widening inequality within and between countries, since only prepared economies capture the gains.
- [[MT212 - Why Cooperation Is Essential in Crisis - IMF Managing Director Kristalina Georgieva#^MT212-C05-3|MT212-C05-3]] — AI investment is supported by actual profits of AI firms, which argues against a pure bubble.
- [[MT212 - Why Cooperation Is Essential in Crisis - IMF Managing Director Kristalina Georgieva#^MT212-C05-X1|MT212-C05-X1]] — Countries with cheap (preferably renewable) energy and high R&D investment have the comparative advantage to push the AI frontier, which links AI capacity to energy cost and research spending.
- [[MT212 - Why Cooperation Is Essential in Crisis - IMF Managing Director Kristalina Georgieva#^MT212-C06-2|MT212-C06-2]] — AI's energy demand threatens to pull countries off the decarbonization path, a larger issue than valuations.

## [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35]]
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C02-1|MT213-C02-1]] — Better data and technology mean the large concentrated discretionary macro bets of the 1990s no longer exist; macro investing has become more systematic and nimble.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C11-2|MT213-C11-2]] — AI follows past technology waves: it eliminates some jobs but creates others and boosts productivity.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C11-X1|MT213-C11-X1]] — Building and calibrating AI models is expensive, and nobody may yet be making money from them, so the productivity benefit arrives with high cost and uncertain commercial payoff.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C13-X2|MT213-C13-X2]] — Markets are non-stationary because participants' behavior changes over decades, so relationships found in any one sample need continuous monitoring and may not persist.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C15-3|MT213-C15-3]] — Emerging markets have been ignored and changes in earnings plus long-term growth make institutions ask about them as a strategic opportunity.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C16-1|MT213-C16-1]] — New technologies such as quantum computing take long to move from lab to robust commercial scale, as with semiconductors.

## [[MT214 - Why Macro Investing Matters More Than Ever - Kenneth Tropin on Markets, Volatility & Oppor]]
- [[MT214 - Why Macro Investing Matters More Than Ever - Kenneth Tropin on Markets, Volatility & Oppor#^MT214-C05-1|MT214-C05-1]] — Discretionary macro relies on people and is harder to scale than quant.
- [[MT214 - Why Macro Investing Matters More Than Ever - Kenneth Tropin on Markets, Volatility & Oppor#^MT214-C07-X1|MT214-C07-X1]] — Because the attempt to broker peace in Eastern Europe and the Middle East has not succeeded, geopolitical conflict will stay relevant to markets for some time, supporting a lasting role for macro trading.

## [[MT215 - Why Most Investors Fail at Trend Following - Systematic Investor - Ep.404]]
- [[MT215 - Why Most Investors Fail at Trend Following - Systematic Investor - Ep.404#^MT215-C04-1|MT215-C04-1]] — Many new advisors and individual investors are ETF-only, so an ETF wrapper widens distribution; tax benefit is mainly on the equity sleeve.
- [[MT215 - Why Most Investors Fail at Trend Following - Systematic Investor - Ep.404#^MT215-C08-1|MT215-C08-1]] — A diversifier that raises return and lowers risk does not fit style boxes (large cap growth, fixed income active), complicating distribution.
- [[MT215 - Why Most Investors Fail at Trend Following - Systematic Investor - Ep.404#^MT215-C10-1|MT215-C10-1]] — Since 2022 bonds stopped diversifying stocks, so ETF model allocators with only stocks and bonds look for a 3-5% alternative allocation.
- [[MT215 - Why Most Investors Fail at Trend Following - Systematic Investor - Ep.404#^MT215-C11-1|MT215-C11-1]] — Capital-efficiency-focused allocators do not want to sell stocks, which drives demand for full portable-alpha (100% equity plus overlay) products.

## [[MT216 - Why Smart Investors Are Switching to Trend Following - Systematic Investor 334]]
- [[MT216 - Why Smart Investors Are Switching to Trend Following - Systematic Investor 334#^MT216-C02-2|MT216-C02-2]] — Fee cuts at Vanguard continue the race to the bottom in ETF fees, which pressures CTA ETFs, some of which have in fact raised fees.
- [[MT216 - Why Smart Investors Are Switching to Trend Following - Systematic Investor 334#^MT216-C02-X1|MT216-C02-X1]] — DeepSeek-style cheaper model training does not remove the need for GPUs, but it may open AI development to a broader set of players, so the implication for the AI hardware trade is unclear.
- [[MT216 - Why Smart Investors Are Switching to Trend Following - Systematic Investor 334#^MT216-C04-3|MT216-C04-3]] — Equities have shrinking share supply through buybacks while crypto supply of coins expands without limit, so a capped Bitcoin supply does not make crypto broadly scarce or an inflation hedge.
- [[MT216 - Why Smart Investors Are Switching to Trend Following - Systematic Investor 334#^MT216-C07-3|MT216-C07-3]] — Investors are moving from using trend following only as a defensive overlay to a strategic allocation because bonds disappointed and the equity-bond correlation may not fall if inflation tensions persist.
- [[MT216 - Why Smart Investors Are Switching to Trend Following - Systematic Investor 334#^MT216-C09-3|MT216-C09-3]] — The 2000-2020 negative stock-bond correlation was the exception; historically the correlation is mostly positive, so a return to positive correlation is normal.

## [[MT217 - Why This Market “Melt-Up” is Making Me Nervous - The Weekly Wrap]]
- [[MT217 - Why This Market “Melt-Up” is Making Me Nervous - The Weekly Wrap#^MT217-C01-1|MT217-C01-1]] — Lending growth since 2008 sits in opaque private credit, so a new credit cycle is only now beginning and losses are hidden until marks are forced.
- [[MT217 - Why This Market “Melt-Up” is Making Me Nervous - The Weekly Wrap#^MT217-C03-3|MT217-C03-3]] — Payment companies outside Visa and Mastercard are poor long-term investments because competitors invade each other's verticals; Toast halved on competition and AI fears.
- [[MT217 - Why This Market “Melt-Up” is Making Me Nervous - The Weekly Wrap#^MT217-C04-1|MT217-C04-1]] — Strong results after mass layoffs (Block cut over 40% of workforce, Bill up to 30%) lift earnings but the stock is range-bound, so AI-driven cost cuts do not guarantee rerating.
- [[MT217 - Why This Market “Melt-Up” is Making Me Nervous - The Weekly Wrap#^MT217-C04-X2|MT217-C04-X2]] — Independent power producers with nuclear and other carbon-free capacity (Constellation Energy) benefit from AI data-center power demand, yet the stock has gone nowhere in a year, so the AI power theme is not automatically rewarded in price.
- [[MT217 - Why This Market “Melt-Up” is Making Me Nervous - The Weekly Wrap#^MT217-C05-1|MT217-C05-1]] — Cisco beat on EPS and revenue and guided higher on AI orders, confirming AI infrastructure demand beyond chips.
- [[MT217 - Why This Market “Melt-Up” is Making Me Nervous - The Weekly Wrap#^MT217-C05-X1|MT217-C05-X1]] — FICO's scoring monopoly is eroding after a 1,500% price rise in five years and a pricing war; if it loses the monopoly mortgage costs should fall, which is why the speaker is short FICO yet not bearish on mortgages or home builders.

## [[MT218 - Why Trend Following Is Harder Than It Looks - Systematic Investor - Ep.405]]
- [[MT218 - Why Trend Following Is Harder Than It Looks - Systematic Investor - Ep.405#^MT218-C01-1|MT218-C01-1]] — Index providers are relaxing eligibility rules (profitability, free float) to admit SpaceX, which changes what index-based benchmarks and CTA indices represent.
- [[MT218 - Why Trend Following Is Harder Than It Looks - Systematic Investor - Ep.405#^MT218-C02-2|MT218-C02-2]] — Reworking or removing government survey data would hurt economic-trend and systematic global macro strategies that use economic releases, while pure price-based trend followers are unaffected.
- [[MT218 - Why Trend Following Is Harder Than It Looks - Systematic Investor - Ep.405#^MT218-C02-3|MT218-C02-3]] — US statistical data quality has degraded (cuts, secrecy, delayed releases from the government shutdown), which weakens economic indicators used as forecasting inputs.
- [[MT218 - Why Trend Following Is Harder Than It Looks - Systematic Investor - Ep.405#^MT218-C03-1|MT218-C03-1]] — Third-party nowcasting services can replace official statistics only if raw data remains available; deliberately removing data is dangerous for models trading on it.
- [[MT218 - Why Trend Following Is Harder Than It Looks - Systematic Investor - Ep.405#^MT218-C03-R1|MT218-C03-R1]] — Carver believes more data is always better, so deliberately ending collection of official statistics is dangerous for the economy and irritating for computers trading on it.
- [[MT218 - Why Trend Following Is Harder Than It Looks - Systematic Investor - Ep.405#^MT218-C08-2|MT218-C08-2]] — Trend following adds access to markets investors cannot easily trade themselves and is unbiased about long or short, e.g. fully short equities in a prolonged crisis.
- [[MT218 - Why Trend Following Is Harder Than It Looks - Systematic Investor - Ep.405#^MT218-C09-1|MT218-C09-1]] — The CFTC approved the first perpetual futures contract (crypto), which is effectively a daily-settled future with no expiry.
- [[MT218 - Why Trend Following Is Harder Than It Looks - Systematic Investor - Ep.405#^MT218-C10-1|MT218-C10-1]] — Perpetual futures are daily-settled futures whose cash flows equal variation margin, so futures price tracks spot (interest, dividend and storage costs are negligible) and no rolling is needed.
- [[MT218 - Why Trend Following Is Harder Than It Looks - Systematic Investor - Ep.405#^MT218-C11-1|MT218-C11-1]] — Daily-settled instruments are cheaper for day traders since they avoid overnight funding costs, while exchange trading means only bid-ask costs.
- [[MT218 - Why Trend Following Is Harder Than It Looks - Systematic Investor - Ep.405#^MT218-C11-2|MT218-C11-2]] — Perpetual futures would cut volume (no rolls) for exchanges; CME sued the CFTC arguing they are swaps, and hedgers like farmers need dated futures for price discovery.
- [[MT218 - Why Trend Following Is Harder Than It Looks - Systematic Investor - Ep.405#^MT218-C14-1|MT218-C14-1]] — Dominance of one asset class in trend returns is a single-factor story (e.g. predictable rate cuts and hikes), not diversification, so shifting into the hot sector reduces diversification.
- [[MT218 - Why Trend Following Is Harder Than It Looks - Systematic Investor - Ep.405#^MT218-C15-1|MT218-C15-1]] — The 2010-19 fixed-income-driven trend period, with zero rates and central banks suppressing inflation, was the exception; earlier decades and the 2020s show more diverse contribution including commodities.
- [[MT218 - Why Trend Following Is Harder Than It Looks - Systematic Investor - Ep.405#^MT218-C15-X1|MT218-C15-X1]] — Over 20-25 years, even through the long equity bull market, equities have not been a strong contributor to trend following P&L, so the financial-sector bias of many CTAs has not been rewarded by equities.
- [[MT218 - Why Trend Following Is Harder Than It Looks - Systematic Investor - Ep.405#^MT218-C15-X2|MT218-C15-X2]] — CTAs have drifted toward liquid financial markets because capacity lets them run more assets and managers are paid on assets managed, an incentive that works against the more diversified commodity-heavy portfolio the speaker favours.

## [[MT219 - Why Trend Following Might Outlast the AI Arms Race - Mark Rzepczynski - Systematic Investo]]
- [[MT219 - Why Trend Following Might Outlast the AI Arms Race - Mark Rzepczynski - Systematic Investo#^MT219-C03-1|MT219-C03-1]] — The belief that trend following only profits when bonds rally (Niederhoffer) reflected the 1981-2020 secular decline in yields; with a return to normal, bond markets are two-way and shorts can pay.
- [[MT219 - Why Trend Following Might Outlast the AI Arms Race - Mark Rzepczynski - Systematic Investo#^MT219-C05-1|MT219-C05-1]] — Hall and Woodford won the Nobel for micro foundations of macro; trend followers should analogously study the micro foundations of markets: who trades, exchange rules and how policy changes profit-maximizing behavior.
- [[MT219 - Why Trend Following Might Outlast the AI Arms Race - Mark Rzepczynski - Systematic Investo#^MT219-C07-1|MT219-C07-1]] — Post-WWII globalization relied on a dominant dollar and institutions (World Bank, IMF) that mitigated dislocations; trend followers should be agnostic about globalization and just exploit the environment.
- [[MT219 - Why Trend Following Might Outlast the AI Arms Race - Mark Rzepczynski - Systematic Investo#^MT219-C08-X1|MT219-C08-X1]] — Globalization may have been a result of the benign 1990s economy (similar monetary policy across countries) rather than its cause, so a benign period followed by crisis is the setting for crisis alpha, not something globalization itself created.
- [[MT219 - Why Trend Following Might Outlast the AI Arms Race - Mark Rzepczynski - Systematic Investo#^MT219-C09-2|MT219-C09-2]] — The bond sell-off is a return to normal rates after two decades of near-zero and negative yields, which were the oddity.
- [[MT219 - Why Trend Following Might Outlast the AI Arms Race - Mark Rzepczynski - Systematic Investo#^MT219-C09-X3|MT219-C09-X3]] — The fair real rate equals the natural rate, i.e. trend growth (about 2%) plus productivity, so a growth and productivity boom (Atlanta Fed GDPNow above 3%) argues for a real rate of 2-2.5% rather than near zero.
- [[MT219 - Why Trend Following Might Outlast the AI Arms Race - Mark Rzepczynski - Systematic Investo#^MT219-C12-1|MT219-C12-1]] — The convenience yield has disappeared at the long end of the Treasury curve and survives mainly in bills, explaining heavier bill issuance.
- [[MT219 - Why Trend Following Might Outlast the AI Arms Race - Mark Rzepczynski - Systematic Investo#^MT219-C12-2|MT219-C12-2]] — Central bank reserve managers are reducing dollar exposure toward multi-polarity, diminishing the dollar's convenience yield relatively.
- [[MT219 - Why Trend Following Might Outlast the AI Arms Race - Mark Rzepczynski - Systematic Investo#^MT219-C13-1|MT219-C13-1]] — Geo-economic fragmentation (tariffs, sanctions, capital controls) reduces cross-market correlation, raising diversification for trend following; less fragmentation raises correlation, so correlation matrices must reflect geoeconomics.
- [[MT219 - Why Trend Following Might Outlast the AI Arms Race - Mark Rzepczynski - Systematic Investo#^MT219-C13-3|MT219-C13-3]] — Fragmentation creates value in trading the same commodity at different locations (London vs New York copper, cocoa) because delivery points and tariffs cause divergence.
- [[MT219 - Why Trend Following Might Outlast the AI Arms Race - Mark Rzepczynski - Systematic Investo#^MT219-C14-2|MT219-C14-2]] — Market-specific 'crisis alpha' arises in markets with few substitutes (diesel, cocoa) and low inventories, so look at substitutability to find nonlinear spikes.
- [[MT219 - Why Trend Following Might Outlast the AI Arms Race - Mark Rzepczynski - Systematic Investo#^MT219-C15-2|MT219-C15-2]] — Trend following has no alpha half-life since it waits for dislocations; low returns while waiting are the cost of doing business.
- [[MT219 - Why Trend Following Might Outlast the AI Arms Race - Mark Rzepczynski - Systematic Investo#^MT219-C16-2|MT219-C16-2]] — Trend following is distinct from ML alpha seeking; use ML to capture trends but ensure the strategy outlasts alpha crowding.

## [[MT220 - Why Trend Following Never Looks the Same Twice - Systematic Investor - Ep.365]]
- [[MT220 - Why Trend Following Never Looks the Same Twice - Systematic Investor - Ep.365#^MT220-C05-X1|MT220-C05-X1]] — Allocation choices create visible manager dispersion: in 2025 CTA ETFs that did not trade equities did relatively well, an example of asset-class tilt driving relative performance in a given year.
- [[MT220 - Why Trend Following Never Looks the Same Twice - Systematic Investor - Ep.365#^MT220-C06-3|MT220-C06-3]] — Man AHL found outperformance and correlation benefits from alternative markets, though depending on the horizon.
- [[MT220 - Why Trend Following Never Looks the Same Twice - Systematic Investor - Ep.365#^MT220-C12-1|MT220-C12-1]] — Quantica compared 50 traditional with 120 alternative markets (credit, gas and power, livestock); sector composition explains recent differences, and alternative markets had extreme outperformance in specific sectors during 2020-22 rather than structurally trending better.
- [[MT220 - Why Trend Following Never Looks the Same Twice - Systematic Investor - Ep.365#^MT220-C13-1|MT220-C13-1]] — Roughly 40-50% of alternative markets' outperformance came from gas and power, tied to the Ukraine shock, which cannot be replicated going forward.

## [[MT221 - Why Trend Following Still Works in Tough Markets - Systematic Investor - Ep.381]]
- [[MT221 - Why Trend Following Still Works in Tough Markets - Systematic Investor - Ep.381#^MT221-C03-2|MT221-C03-2]] — A trend-following programme already behaves like a total portfolio approach because it dynamically allocates risk to the markets with the best risk-adjusted opportunity rather than holding static weights.
- [[MT221 - Why Trend Following Still Works in Tough Markets - Systematic Investor - Ep.381#^MT221-C05-X1|MT221-C05-X1]] — Most of the investment world runs a simple long-only stock and bond mix whose structure only dates from about 1985, and it persists through recency bias and because the long falling-rate era removed the need to manage risk, so its apparent diversification is untested in a different regime.
- [[MT221 - Why Trend Following Still Works in Tough Markets - Systematic Investor - Ep.381#^MT221-C09-1|MT221-C09-1]] — A replication of the CTA index need not change when underlying managers change models, as long as their changes net out and managers do not become too fast for the replicator to track.
- [[MT221 - Why Trend Following Still Works in Tough Markets - Systematic Investor - Ep.381#^MT221-C15-2|MT221-C15-2]] — The Barclay CTA index volatility fell from 20-25% in the 1980s/early 1990s to below 5% in recent years as institutions entered and assets grew.

## [[MT222 - Why Trend Following Works - Systematic Investor - Ep.391]]
- [[MT222 - Why Trend Following Works - Systematic Investor - Ep.391#^MT222-C07-1|MT222-C07-1]] — Logic test: a memoryless market implies no persistence, normal returns and steady volatility; finding persistence, fat tails and volatility clustering together shows feedback is structural.

## [[MT223 - Why Trend Thrives When Inflation Returns - Systematic Investor - Ep.373]]
- [[MT223 - Why Trend Thrives When Inflation Returns - Systematic Investor - Ep.373#^MT223-C08-X1|MT223-C08-X1]] — More elastic markets have lower volatility because supply or demand absorbs shocks with small price moves; bond volatility is lower than equity volatility for this reason, so elasticity helps explain cross-market differences in trend size.

## [[MT224 - Why the Best Portfolios Are Built to Be Wrong - Systematic Investor - Ep. 411]]
- [[MT224 - Why the Best Portfolios Are Built to Be Wrong - Systematic Investor - Ep. 411#^MT224-C03-1|MT224-C03-1]] — Framing: Dredge (options/convexity) and Brennan (trend following) both treat markets as complex adaptive systems, so Gaussian statistics are the wrong mathematics and risk is not volatility.
- [[MT224 - Why the Best Portfolios Are Built to Be Wrong - Systematic Investor - Ep. 411#^MT224-C04-1|MT224-C04-1]] — Markets are reflexive, non-stationary, computationally irreducible processes where agents react to a system that reacts to them, so outcomes cannot be forecast with linear statistics (Mandelbrot: roughness at every scale).
- [[MT224 - Why the Best Portfolios Are Built to Be Wrong - Systematic Investor - Ep. 411#^MT224-C07-1|MT224-C07-1]] — Price moves because of transactions; fundamentals, macro and flows are downstream, and an ecosystem of participant types (fundamental, trend, convergent, random) applies directed forces whose feedback creates fractal price structure.
- [[MT224 - Why the Best Portfolios Are Built to Be Wrong - Systematic Investor - Ep. 411#^MT224-C08-1|MT224-C08-1]] — The simulation shows a sharp phase transition at about 25% divergent participation rather than a gradual change, so amplifying positions create the tails, memory and volatility clusters seen in real markets.
- [[MT224 - Why the Best Portfolios Are Built to Be Wrong - Systematic Investor - Ep. 411#^MT224-C09-1|MT224-C09-1]] — Citing Bouchaud, about 90% of big market moves come from endogenous sources, not news; activity picks up, liquidity thins, the book fragilises, then a trigger hits and settling takes longer than the orthodoxy assumes.
- [[MT224 - Why the Best Portfolios Are Built to Be Wrong - Systematic Investor - Ep. 411#^MT224-C09-X1|MT224-C09-X1]] — The agent-based model reproduces fat tails, volatility clustering and memory with no news events at all, using only convergent, divergent and random participants, which supports the claim that these properties arise from market structure and not from information arrival.
- [[MT224 - Why the Best Portfolios Are Built to Be Wrong - Systematic Investor - Ep. 411#^MT224-C12-2|MT224-C12-2]] — Calm markets are not equilibrium; quiet is energy building like a stretched rubber band that traditional risk metrics hide.

## [[MT225 - Why the Market Isn’t Moving, And Why That’s Dangerous - Systematic Investor - Ep.389]]
- [[MT225 - Why the Market Isn’t Moving, And Why That’s Dangerous - Systematic Investor - Ep.389#^MT225-C02-3|MT225-C02-3]] — In 2017 realised index volatility was the lowest in 125 years (by 30%) with correlations the lowest in history (by 25%), marking a structural shift in dispersion driven by growth of options and structured products.
- [[MT225 - Why the Market Isn’t Moving, And Why That’s Dangerous - Systematic Investor - Ep.389#^MT225-C06-1|MT225-C06-1]] — AI disruption narrative (Citrini piece) is underpriced for political backlash, friction in corporate adoption and regulatory catch-up, and assumes no global conflict, commodity shortages or liquidity stress; technology adoption is slower than expected historically.

## [[MT226 - Why the Old Rules of Diversification Are Changing - Allocator - Ep.38]]
- [[MT226 - Why the Old Rules of Diversification Are Changing - Allocator - Ep.38#^MT226-C02-1|MT226-C02-1]] — Today's macro regime is driven by supply-side shocks (war, energy, AI capex) rather than the lack of aggregate demand that characterised the 2010s, which changes policy, volatility regime and portfolio construction.
- [[MT226 - Why the Old Rules of Diversification Are Changing - Allocator - Ep.38#^MT226-C04-3|MT226-C04-3]] — A supply-driven world is a world of scarcity (supply chains, energy, inputs), in contrast to the abundance of capital and demand-weakness of the 2010s.
- [[MT226 - Why the Old Rules of Diversification Are Changing - Allocator - Ep.38#^MT226-C04-X1|MT226-C04-X1]] — Hyperscaler earnings that used to fund buybacks are now being deployed into capex, so the market has moved from ample capital to a world of capital constraints (host framing, put to the guest as a question).
- [[MT226 - Why the Old Rules of Diversification Are Changing - Allocator - Ep.38#^MT226-C05-1|MT226-C05-1]] — The AI buildout is creating real-resource bottlenecks (leading-edge chips, energy and other inputs) that add to scarcity.
- [[MT226 - Why the Old Rules of Diversification Are Changing - Allocator - Ep.38#^MT226-C05-3|MT226-C05-3]] — The key macro question is when AI investment shifts the regime from scarcity to abundance via productivity gains.
- [[MT226 - Why the Old Rules of Diversification Are Changing - Allocator - Ep.38#^MT226-C05-X1|MT226-C05-X1]] — The Russia-Ukraine war was the first of two major energy shocks in five years (the second being the Iran war), showing that global energy supply is exposed to geopolitical risk and reinforcing the scarcity, supply-driven regime.
- [[MT226 - Why the Old Rules of Diversification Are Changing - Allocator - Ep.38#^MT226-C06-2|MT226-C06-2]] — Evidence of AI productivity gains is so far fragmentary: incremental investment is producing incremental corporate earnings, but not yet enough to outweigh resource demands of the buildout.
- [[MT226 - Why the Old Rules of Diversification Are Changing - Allocator - Ep.38#^MT226-C07-1|MT226-C07-1]] — The speaker has some confidence in a medium-term disinflationary trend from productivity-enhancing technology.
- [[MT226 - Why the Old Rules of Diversification Are Changing - Allocator - Ep.38#^MT226-C08-1|MT226-C08-1]] — Demand for AI capability still exceeds supply, implying continued buildout and a healthy, though lower than peak, earnings trajectory.
- [[MT226 - Why the Old Rules of Diversification Are Changing - Allocator - Ep.38#^MT226-C12-2|MT226-C12-2]] — The 2010s were poor for hedge funds because cheap diversification (long bonds) was abundant while dispersion and the alpha opportunity set were compressed.
- [[MT226 - Why the Old Rules of Diversification Are Changing - Allocator - Ep.38#^MT226-C12-3|MT226-C12-3]] — Today diversification is scarce and dispersion is larger, which supports hedge fund performance, especially long-short, and allocators returning to them.
- [[MT226 - Why the Old Rules of Diversification Are Changing - Allocator - Ep.38#^MT226-C13-1|MT226-C13-1]] — Concentrated equity markets make it hard for long-only active managers to deliver alpha; adding shorts lets managers exploit dispersion.

## [[MT227 - Yield Storm Ahead- Preparing for a New Era - Systematic Investor 330]]
- [[MT227 - Yield Storm Ahead- Preparing for a New Era - Systematic Investor 330#^MT227-C02-2|MT227-C02-2]] — Populist, protectionist sentiment is a structural trend that Trump accelerates, and the guest sees a regime change comparable to earlier periods, with the 10-year Treasury signalling it.
- [[MT227 - Yield Storm Ahead- Preparing for a New Era - Systematic Investor 330#^MT227-C07-3|MT227-C07-3]] — In the last structurally inflationary period (1968-82) equities lost about 67% in real terms over 14 years; populism and protection are seen as recreating that regime.
- [[MT227 - Yield Storm Ahead- Preparing for a New Era - Systematic Investor 330#^MT227-C10-1|MT227-C10-1]] — Equities are not an inflation hedge during structural inflationary periods, despite what most seasoned professionals say.
- [[MT227 - Yield Storm Ahead- Preparing for a New Era - Systematic Investor 330#^MT227-C13-1|MT227-C13-1]] — Under a fiat reserve currency the Fed's ability to control volatility drove money to corporations and supported globalization and the long period of peace and stability.
- [[MT227 - Yield Storm Ahead- Preparing for a New Era - Systematic Investor 330#^MT227-C13-2|MT227-C13-2]] — As the Fed loses its dominant role, more realized volatility and geopolitical conflict follow as institutions and countries pull levers of power.
- [[MT227 - Yield Storm Ahead- Preparing for a New Era - Systematic Investor 330#^MT227-C13-3|MT227-C13-3]] — Expect more global conflict and breakdown of institutions because power is being questioned.
- [[MT227 - Yield Storm Ahead- Preparing for a New Era - Systematic Investor 330#^MT227-C13-X1|MT227-C13-X1]] — Major global institutions (WTO after China's entry, the WHO, and the EU, whose competitiveness the Draghi report called into question) are seen as failing, leaving a leadership vacuum that adds to geopolitical and market instability.
- [[MT227 - Yield Storm Ahead- Preparing for a New Era - Systematic Investor 330#^MT227-C16-2|MT227-C16-2]] — Narrative-driven political influence will produce more volatility, dispersion and rotations in markets.
